<div class="tg-quote-container"><div class="tg-section-divider"><span>”</span></div><blockquote class="tg-quote noexcerpt">
<p>Gandalf was shorter in stature than the <a href="../E/Elrond.md" title="Elrond">other</a> <a href="Glorfindel.md" title="Glorfindel">two</a>; but his long white hair, his sweeping silver beard, and his broad shoulders, made him look like some wise king of ancient legend. In his aged face under great snowy brows his eyes were set like coals that could suddenly burst into fire.
</p>
<p class="tg-quote-footer"><cite><i><a href="../T/The_Fellowship_of_the_Ring.md" title="The Fellowship of the Ring">The Fellowship of the Ring</a></i>, &#34;<a href="../M/Many_Meetings.md" title="Many Meetings">Many Meetings</a>&#34;</cite></p>
</blockquote><div class="tg-section-divider"></div></div>

**Gandalf** was one of the five [Istari](../O/Order_of_Wizards.md) sent to [Middle-earth](../M/Middle-earth.md) by the [Valar](../V/Valar.md) in the [Third Age](../T/Third_Age.md). In [Valinor](../V/Valinor.md) he was known as Olórin. Gandalf was instrumental in bringing about the demise of [Sauron](../S/Sauron.md) in [T.A.](../T/Third_Age.md) [3019](../T/Third_Age_3019.md), chiefly by encouraging others and dispensing his wisdom at pivotal times. He was originally robed in grey, and second to [Saruman](../S/Saruman.md) in the Order of Wizards. After his fall in [Moria](../M/Moria.md), Gandalf returned to Middle-earth as head of the Order, robed in white. He was noteworthy for his keen interest in [Hobbits](../H/Hobbits.md).

## History {#History}

### Early history {#Early_history}

<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/K/Kip_Rasmussen_-_Olorin_in_the_Gardens_of_Lorien.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/b/b0/Kip_Rasmussen_-_Olorin_in_the_Gardens_of_Lorien.jpg/300px-Kip_Rasmussen_-_Olorin_in_the_Gardens_of_Lorien.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="300" height="450" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/b/b0/Kip_Rasmussen_-_Olorin_in_the_Gardens_of_Lorien.jpg/450px-Kip_Rasmussen_-_Olorin_in_the_Gardens_of_Lorien.jpg 1.5x, https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/b/b0/Kip_Rasmussen_-_Olorin_in_the_Gardens_of_Lorien.jpg/600px-Kip_Rasmussen_-_Olorin_in_the_Gardens_of_Lorien.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="1536"/></a><figcaption><i>Olorin in the Gardens of Lorien</i> by <a href="../../category/I/Images_by_Kip_Rasmussen.md" title="Category:Images by Kip Rasmussen">Kip Rasmussen</a></figcaption></figure>

The wisest of the [Maiar](../M/Maiar.md), Gandalf was created by [Ilúvatar](../I/Ilúvatar.md) before the [Music of the Ainur](../M/Music_of_the_Ainur.md).<sup id="cite_ref-Ainu_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ainu-4">[4]</a></sup> At the beginning of [Time](../T/Time.md), he was amongst the [Ainur](../A/Ainur.md) who entered into [Eä](../E/Eä.md).<sup id="cite_ref-SVala_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SVala-5">[5]</a></sup> In his "youth" he was known as <i><b>Olórin</b></i> and was a counsellor of [Irmo](../I/Irmo.md).<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">[6]</a></sup> He lived in [Lórien](<../L/Lórien_(Valinor).md>), yet his ways often took him to Nienna, from whom he learned pity and patience.<sup id="cite_ref-vala_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vala-7">[7]</a></sup> He became one of the [Maiar](../M/Maiar.md) who served [Manwë](../M/Manwë.md), [Varda](../V/Varda.md), [Irmo](../I/Irmo.md), and [Nienna](../N/Nienna.md). He was associated with light and fire, much like [Varda](../V/Varda.md).

After the Valar discovered the primeval [Elves](../E/Elves.md) at [Cuiviénen](../C/Cuiviénen.md), Olórin was sent there as one of the [Five Guardians](../F/Five_Guardians.md) to guard the Elves from the forces of [Melkor](../M/Melkor.md).<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">[8]</a></sup> It is unclear how long he remained or if he accompanied the [Eldar](../E/Eldar.md) on the [Great Journey](Great_Journey.md). But it is said he loved the Elves, and throughout his early life, he walked [Unseen](../U/Unseen.md) among them, or wore a [Fana](../F/Fana.md) that appeared like an Elf, and he sent them fair visions in their hearts that made them wiser. Throughout his existence, he followed the example of Nienna, taking pity on the sorrows of the [Children of Ilúvatar](../C/Children_of_Ilúvatar.md) and lifting out of despair those who listened to him.<sup id="cite_ref-vala_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vala-7">[7]</a></sup>

### Third Age {#Third_Age}

#### Arrival in Middle-earth {#Arrival_in_Middle-earth}

During the [Third Age](../T/Third_Age.md), while [Sauron's](../S/Sauron.md) 'dark spirit of malice' was increasing over [Middle-earth](../M/Middle-earth.md), the Valar chose the [Istari](../I/Istari.md), missionaries from among the Maiar, to go to Middle-earth and help the [peoples](../../portal/C/Characters.md). [Manwë](../M/Manwë.md) selected Olórin, who, at first, did not wish to go, as he feared Sauron. However, Manwë said that it was all the more reason why he should go.<sup id="cite_ref-Istari_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Istari-9">[9]</a></sup> Furthermore, [the One Ring](../T/The_One_Ring.md), the location of much of Sauron's power, presumably still existed somewhere in Middle-earth.

Olórin submitted to the will of Manwë and departed Valinor to [Mithlond](../M/Mithlond.md) in the year [T.A.](../T/Third_Age.md) [1000](../T/Third_Age_1000.md).<sup id="cite_ref-Istari_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Istari-9">[9]</a></sup> He arrived shortly after two other Istari, [Curumo](../C/Curumo.md) and [Aiwendil](../A/Aiwendil.md), at about the same time the [Necromancer](../N/Necromancer.md) appeared in [Mirkwood](../M/Mirkwood.md). Like the other Wizards, he took the shape of an old man. He was robed in grey.

At Mithlond, he was welcomed by [Círdan](../C/Círdan.md) the shipwright, who possessed [Narya](../N/Narya.md), one of the [Three](../T/Three_Rings.md) Elven [Rings of Power](../R/Rings_of_Power.md). Círdan divined in Olórin a sense of strength and power despite his appearance as a bent and aged old man. Círdan gave Narya to Olórin with a prediction of his future struggles with evil, and a promise that it would support and aid him in his labours. Then Círdan said "*But as for me, my heart is with the [Sea](../B/Belegaer.md), and I will dwell by the grey shores until the last ship sails. I will await you.*"<sup id="cite_ref-ThirdAge_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ThirdAge-10">[10]</a></sup>

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/J/John_Howe_-_Gandalf_the_Grey.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/a/a6/John_Howe_-_Gandalf_the_Grey.jpg/300px-John_Howe_-_Gandalf_the_Grey.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="300" height="416" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/a/a6/John_Howe_-_Gandalf_the_Grey.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="394" data-file-height="546"/></a><figcaption><i>Gandalf the Grey</i> by <a href="../J/John_Howe.md" title="John Howe">John Howe</a></figcaption></figure>

Gandalf then began his sojourn in Middle-earth, going about as a wanderer and counsellor. Unlike Saruman, Gandalf did not take up a single permanent residence. Gandalf never went to the [east](../E/East.md)<sup id="cite_ref-TT_Window_p._670_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TT_Window_p._670-11">[11]</a></sup> and apparently restricted his activities to the [Westlands](../W/Westlands.md) of Middle-earth, where the remnants of the [Dúnedain](../D/Dúnedain.md) and the [Eldar](../E/Eldar.md) remained to oppose Sauron. He was known by many names during the long years he remained in Middle-earth: Elves named him *Mithrandir*, the Grey Pilgrim, while the men of [Arnor](../A/Arnor.md) named him **Gandalf**, which became his most common name. He was also known as *Incánus* in the south, and *Tharkûn* to the [Dwarves](../D/Dwarves.md).<sup id="cite_ref-Istari_9-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Istari-9">[9]</a></sup>

A legend says that Gandalf was given the [Elfstone](../E/Elfstone.md) of [Eärendil](../E/Eärendil.md) by [Yavanna](../Y/Yavanna.md) to bring to the peoples of Middle-earth; this was to be a token that the Valar had not forsaken them. He gave it to [Galadriel](Galadriel.md), and remarked prophetically that she would in turn pass it to an individual who would also be called [Elessar](../A/Aragorn.md).<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">[12]</a></sup>

#### Early vigilance {#Early_vigilance}

Around [T.A.](../T/Third_Age.md) [1100](../T/Third_Age_1100.md),<sup id="cite_ref-ThirdAge_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ThirdAge-10">[10]</a></sup> the Istari and the Eldar discovered that some evil entity resided at [Dol Guldur](../D/Dol_Guldur.md) in Mirkwood. Dark shapes moved in the forest and evil began to multiply in the woods. Some thought a [Nazgûl](../N/Nazgûl.md) had returned to torment the world, or some new evil was arising. Gandalf was unsure, and feared that perhaps Sauron himself might have returned.<sup id="cite_ref-Rings_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rings-13">[13]</a></sup>

<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/J/John_Howe_-_Pass_the_Doors_of_Dol_Guldur.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/d/d0/John_Howe_-_Pass_the_Doors_of_Dol_Guldur.jpg/300px-John_Howe_-_Pass_the_Doors_of_Dol_Guldur.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="300" height="252" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/d/d0/John_Howe_-_Pass_the_Doors_of_Dol_Guldur.jpg/450px-John_Howe_-_Pass_the_Doors_of_Dol_Guldur.jpg 1.5x, https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/d/d0/John_Howe_-_Pass_the_Doors_of_Dol_Guldur.jpg 2x" data-file-width="534" data-file-height="448"/></a><figcaption><i>Pass the Doors of Dol Guldur</i> by <a href="../J/John_Howe.md" title="John Howe">John Howe</a></figcaption></figure>

Over the next two hundred years the evil continued to grow, as well as the source directing it. [Orcs](../O/Orcs.md) were multiplying in the [Misty Mountains](../M/Misty_Mountains.md) and elsewhere. The [Witch-king](../W/Witch-king.md), the mightiest of the Nazgûl, had built a fortress in [Angmar](../A/Angmar.md) in the Northern wastelands and waged unending war against the Kingdom of [Arnor](../A/Arnor.md). Meanwhile [Moria](../M/Moria.md) and [Minas Ithil](../M/Minas_Ithil.md) fell under a shadow, while other wars, plagues, and catastrophes occurred across Middle-earth. Gandalf went to Dol Guldur in [2063](../T/Third_Age_2063.md) to discover its secret.<sup id="cite_ref-ThirdAge_10-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ThirdAge-10">[10]</a></sup> An entity known only as the '[Necromancer](../N/Necromancer.md)' fled before him preventing Gandalf from identifying him.<sup id="cite_ref-Rings_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rings-13">[13]</a></sup> After Gandalf's incursion, the evil there seemed to desist, and its absence allowed for some centuries of calmness.

That peaceful period was known as the [Watchful Peace](../W/Watchful_Peace.md) and lasted for almost four hundred years. However, the Nazgûl had used this period to prepare for Sauron's return, which occurred in [2460](../T/Third_Age_2460.md).<sup id="cite_ref-ThirdAge_10-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ThirdAge-10">[10]</a></sup> Realising the returning evil, the [Wise](../W/Wise.md) formed the [White Council](../W/White_Council.md) three years later. [Galadriel](Galadriel.md), bearer of one of the Three Elven Rings and mighty among the Eldar, wished Gandalf to be the chief of the Council, but he refused the position wishing to set down no roots and to maintain his independence; Saruman took this place because of his vast knowledge.<sup id="cite_ref-Rings_13-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rings-13">[13]</a></sup>

<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/M/Miriam_Ellis_-_The_Long_Winter,_1158_-_Gandalf_Helps_the_Hobbits.jpeg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/f/f5/Miriam_Ellis_-_The_Long_Winter%2C_1158_-_Gandalf_Helps_the_Hobbits.jpeg/250px-Miriam_Ellis_-_The_Long_Winter%2C_1158_-_Gandalf_Helps_the_Hobbits.jpeg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="250" height="208" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/f/f5/Miriam_Ellis_-_The_Long_Winter%2C_1158_-_Gandalf_Helps_the_Hobbits.jpeg/375px-Miriam_Ellis_-_The_Long_Winter%2C_1158_-_Gandalf_Helps_the_Hobbits.jpeg 1.5x, https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/f/f5/Miriam_Ellis_-_The_Long_Winter%2C_1158_-_Gandalf_Helps_the_Hobbits.jpeg/500px-Miriam_Ellis_-_The_Long_Winter%2C_1158_-_Gandalf_Helps_the_Hobbits.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="832"/></a><figcaption><i><a href="../L/Long_Winter.md" title="Long Winter">The Long Winter</a>, <a href="../T/Third_Age_2758.md" title="Third Age 2758">1158</a>: Gandalf Helps the <a href="../H/Hobbits.md" title="Hobbits">Hobbits</a></i> by <a href="../M/Miriam_Ellis.md" title="Miriam Ellis">Miriam Ellis</a></figcaption></figure>

During his wanderings in [Eriador](../E/Eriador.md), Gandalf met and befriended the isolated and secretive people of the [Hobbits](../H/Hobbits.md) in their country, [The Shire](../T/The_Shire.md). During the [Long Winter](../L/Long_Winter.md) of [2758](../T/Third_Age_2758.md) Gandalf came to their aid.<sup id="cite_ref-ThirdAge_10-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ThirdAge-10">[10]</a></sup> It was then when he witnessed and admired the pity and courage the humble Hobbits reserved in their hearts.<sup id="cite_ref-Erebor_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Erebor-14">[14]</a></sup>

When King [Thráin II](../T/Thráin.md), a dwarf of the royal line of [Lonely Mountain](../L/Lonely_Mountain.md), disappeared on a journey to [Erebor](../E/Erebor.md), Gandalf looked for him. At some point after [2845](../T/Third_Age_2845.md) he entered the abandoned city of Khazad-dûm. After his vain search, the Wizard exited through the [Doors of Durin](../D/Doors_of_Durin.md); however this experience did not help him know how to open the doors from the outside.<sup id="cite_ref-journey_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-journey-15">[15]</a></sup>

In [2850](../T/Third_Age_2850.md), his quest led him once more to Dol Guldur, this time in secrecy.<sup id="cite_ref-ThirdAge_10-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ThirdAge-10">[10]</a></sup> He found Thráin in the dungeons, who gave the Wizard his last possessions, the [map](../T/Thrór's_Map.md) and key to Erebor.<sup id="cite_ref-Durin_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Durin-16">[16]</a></sup> Most importantly, Gandalf found that the Necromancer was no Nazgûl, but was Sauron himself, and he had taken [the last](../R/Ring_of_Thrór.md) of the [Seven Rings](../S/Seven_Rings.md) from the Dwarf King. Sauron was gathering the remaining [Rings of Power](../R/Rings_of_Power.md) and possibly searching for his lost [One Ring](../T/The_One_Ring.md).

Gandalf escaped Dol Guldur and returned to the White Council.<sup id="cite_ref-Rings_13-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rings-13">[13]</a></sup> After relating his discoveries, he urged the council to attack Sauron while the One Ring was still lost and Sauron's power immature. But Saruman said that it was better to watch and wait, and that the One Ring had long ago rolled from [Anduin](../A/Anduin.md) to the Sea.<sup id="cite_ref-Rings_13-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rings-13">[13]</a></sup> The majority of the council agreed with Saruman. [Elrond](../E/Elrond.md) Half-elven, a powerful member of the council, later privately told Gandalf he had a foreboding that the Ring would be found, and that the war to end the [Age](../A/Ages.md) was coming. Indeed, he added, he feared that it would end in darkness and despair. Gandalf encouraged him, saying there were many "strange chances," and that, "*help oft shall come from the hands of the weak when the Wise falter*".<sup id="cite_ref-Rings_13-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rings-13">[13]</a></sup> Gandalf did not yet realise that Saruman now wanted the Ring for himself and was secretly searching for it along the banks of the river Anduin.

#### Quest for Erebor {#Quest_for_Erebor}

Gandalf would visit the Shire occasionally and participate in the [Midsummer](../M/Midsummer.md)-eve parties of the [Old Took](../O/Old_Took.md) where he impressed young Hobbits with his fireworks, and his stories about [Dragons](../D/Dragons.md), [Goblins](../O/Orcs.md#Orcs_and_Goblins) and princesses.<sup id="cite_ref-Erebor_14-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Erebor-14">[14]</a></sup> He was thus known to be "responsible for so many quiet lads and lasses going off into the Blue for mad adventures". He also met a relatively adventurous Hobbit named [Bilbo Baggins](../B/Bilbo_Baggins.md) although he was far from "adventuresome."<sup id="cite_ref-Party_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Party-3">[3]</a></sup>

Gandalf was concerned about the weak state of the North. [Smaug](../S/Smaug.md) the [Dragon](../D/Dragons.md) had destroyed both the [Kingdom under the Mountain](../K/Kingdom_under_the_Mountain.md) and the town of [Dale](../D/Dale.md), and Gandalf feared that [Sauron](../S/Sauron.md) might use the desolation around Erebor to regain the northern passes in the mountains and the old lands of Angmar. Gandalf knew that exiled Dwarf [King of Durin's Folk](../K/King_of_Durin's_Folk~7047.md) [Thorin Oakenshield](../T/Thorin_Oakenshield.md) planned to battle against Smaug, but he knew that it would not be enough. In [2941](../T/Third_Age_2941.md),<sup id="cite_ref-ThirdAge_10-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ThirdAge-10">[10]</a></sup> while staying the night in [Bree](../B/Bree.md), Gandalf happened across the Dwarf King.<sup id="cite_ref-Erebor_14-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Erebor-14">[14]</a></sup> Thorin initiated conversation; he had been having a strange feeling urging him to seek Gandalf. Gandalf was intrigued, for he had thought to seek Thorin as well. They found they were taking the same road for a while (Thorin passing through [the Shire](../T/The_Shire.md) on his way to the [Ered Luin](../B/Blue_Mountains.md)), and they agreed to travel together. Thorin wanted advice, and Gandalf in turn wanted to discuss the Dragon Smaug with Thorin.

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/D/David_T._Wenzel_-_Good_Morning_Mr._Baggins.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/b/b5/David_T._Wenzel_-_Good_Morning_Mr._Baggins.jpg/300px-David_T._Wenzel_-_Good_Morning_Mr._Baggins.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="300" height="446" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/b/b5/David_T._Wenzel_-_Good_Morning_Mr._Baggins.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="336" data-file-height="500"/></a><figcaption><i>Good Morning Mr. Baggins</i> by <a href="../D/David_T._Wenzel.md" title="David T. Wenzel">David T. Wenzel</a></figcaption></figure>

Ultimately, Gandalf concocted a plan wherein Thorin could destroy Smaug and recover his family fortune, albeit with a "burglar" of Gandalf's own choosing. Gandalf had a feeling that a Hobbit should be involved, and he remembered Bilbo Baggins. At first he found only [Holman Cotton](../H/Holman_Cotton.md) as Bilbo had left on the occasion of the [Elven new year](../Y/Yestare.md), something that persuaded Gandalf that Bilbo was the right person for the job.<sup id="cite_ref-Erebor_14-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Erebor-14">[14]</a></sup> He visited Bilbo later, bringing along the kinsmen of Thorin.<sup id="cite_ref-Party_3-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Party-3">[3]</a></sup> In the end Gandalf convinced the reluctant Baggins to become a burglar for Thorin.

Gandalf accompanied [Thorin and Company](../T/Thorin_and_Company.md) to [Rivendell](../R/Rivendell.md). Gandalf saved them from a trio of [Stone-trolls](../S/Stone-trolls.md), and later obtained the legendary sword [Glamdring](Glamdring.md) from their [Troll](../T/Trolls.md)-hoard; Gandalf bore it thenceforth.<sup id="cite_ref-Mutton_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mutton-17">[17]</a></sup> He also helped Thorin and Company through the [Misty Mountains](../M/Misty_Mountains.md). When they unknowingly camped in the [Front Porch](../F/Front_Porch.md) of the [Goblin-town](Goblin-town.md), the Goblins captured the whole company except Gandalf, who surprised them much later by killing the [Great Goblin](Great_Goblin.md) and then rescuing the Dwarves and leading them to the exit.<sup id="cite_ref-Hill_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hill-18">[18]</a></sup> It was during this time that Bilbo obtained a "magic ring".<sup id="cite_ref-Riddles_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Riddles-19">[19]</a></sup> The hobbit initially claimed he "won" it from the creature "[Gollum](Gollum.md)" while the company was under the Misty Mountains,<sup id="cite_ref-Pan_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pan-20">[20]</a></sup> and he kept it secret from Gandalf for some time.

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/D/Darrell_Sweet_-_The_Lord_of_the_Eagles.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/6/60/Darrell_Sweet_-_The_Lord_of_the_Eagles.jpg/300px-Darrell_Sweet_-_The_Lord_of_the_Eagles.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="300" height="279" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/6/60/Darrell_Sweet_-_The_Lord_of_the_Eagles.jpg/450px-Darrell_Sweet_-_The_Lord_of_the_Eagles.jpg 1.5x, https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/6/60/Darrell_Sweet_-_The_Lord_of_the_Eagles.jpg/600px-Darrell_Sweet_-_The_Lord_of_the_Eagles.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1195" data-file-height="1111"/></a><figcaption><i>The Lord of the Eagles</i> by <a href="../D/Darrell_Sweet.md" title="Darrell Sweet">Darrell Sweet</a></figcaption></figure>

On their escape out, the Company was saved by the [Eagles](../E/Eagles.md) of the Misty Mountains; Gandalf once had healed the [Great Eagle](Great_Eagle.md) from a poisoned wound,<sup id="cite_ref-Pan_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pan-20">[20]</a></sup> and thenceforth they became friends. The Eagles picked them up and flew them to their eyrie. The next day, they dropped them to the other side of the Mountains.<sup id="cite_ref-Pan_20-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pan-20">[20]</a></sup>

Gandalf then sought the hospitality of [Beorn](../B/Beorn.md), persuading him to welcome and host the whole Company in his [hall](../B/Beorn's_Hall.md). But then he left the quest prior to its completion; after leading the Company to the outskirts of the [Forest Gate](../F/Forest_Gate.md), he gave them some final advice (but little did he know that the lands had changed by then) before they entered [Mirkwood](../M/Mirkwood.md), and went to attend other obligations.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">[21]</a></sup> The [White Council](../W/White_Council.md) had met under the gravest of circumstances: Sauron's vast power was returning, even without his Ring. Gandalf at last convinced the Council to attack Dol Guldur, to which even Saruman agreed (as by now he feared Sauron as a rival, and wished to delay his search for the Ring). Gandalf joined his peers in assailing [Dol Guldur](../D/Dol_Guldur.md), ridding [Mirkwood](../M/Mirkwood.md) of the Necromancer's presence, who fled to [Mordor](../M/Mordor.md).<sup id="cite_ref-ThirdAge_10-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ThirdAge-10">[10]</a></sup> When he was about to finish his task, news about what happened to Thorin's Company in Mirkwood reached him, and he realised that the instructions he gave them did not help; they had lost their way, and then vanished from the imprisonment of the [Wood-elves](../S/Silvan_Elves.md), who had captured them. He was anxious to get back to them as soon as possible.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">[22]</a></sup>

Meanwhile Thorin's quest was successful: Erebor was retaken and Smaug was killed,<sup id="cite_ref-Fire_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fire-23">[23]</a></sup> but when Gandalf finally arrived to the area, he found the [Dwarves of Erebor](../D/Dwarves_of_Erebor.md) and [the Iron Hills](../D/Dwarves_of_the_Iron_Hills.md) preparing for an attack by the [Lake-men](../L/Lake-men.md) and the [Elves of Mirkwood](../E/Elves_of_Mirkwood.md). He was with [Bard](../B/Bard.md) and [Thranduil](../T/Thranduil.md) and revealed his presence trying to reason with Thorin. When the attack was beginning, Gandalf halted them, to warn that the [Orcs](../O/Orcs.md) and [Wargs](../W/Wargs.md) were coming to claim the treasure. He invited [Dáin Ironfoot](../D/Dáin_Ironfoot.md) for council, and soon Dwarves, Elves and Men formed an alliance, and defeated the [Orcs of the Misty Mountains](../O/Orcs_of_the_Misty_Mountains.md) in the [Battle of Five Armies](../B/Battle_of_Five_Armies.md).<sup id="cite_ref-Burst_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Burst-24">[24]</a></sup>

King Thorin was mortally wounded and after his funeral and the reestablishment of Erebor under Dáin, Bilbo and Gandalf left Erebor for the Shire; on the way, they celebrated [Yule](../Y/Yule.md) at Beorn's, and then returned to Rivendell, where Gandalf discussed with [Elrond](../E/Elrond.md) the events of Dol Guldur and the Lonely Mountain. Gandalf had accomplished his immediate goal, which was to destroy Smaug, who could have been used to disastrous effect by Sauron. A large number of Orcs and Wargs also were killed in the North, removing threats to Rivendell and Lothlórien.<sup id="cite_ref-Erebor_14-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Erebor-14">[14]</a></sup> But they both agreed that it would be better if the Necromancer were banished from the world altogether.<sup id="cite_ref-H19_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-H19-25">[25]</a></sup>

As Gandalf and Bilbo passed by the site of their former encounter with the stone-trolls, they recovered the gold of the troll-hoard they had buried.<sup id="cite_ref-H19_25-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-H19-25">[25]</a></sup>

Gandalf was amazed by the hobbit; until then he had paid no attention to hobbits and knew little of them. For the rest of his sojourn in Middle-earth, Gandalf took a special interest in hobbits, and particularly in the Baggins family. [Years later](../T/Third_Age_2949.md), he and Balin paid a visit to Bilbo, discussing the news from Erebor and Dale.<sup id="cite_ref-ThirdAge_10-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ThirdAge-10">[10]</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-H19_25-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-H19-25">[25]</a></sup>

#### Return of the Shadow {#Return_of_the_Shadow}

Despite the Council's hopes, Sauron was not weakened by this attack. He had foreseen the move that drove him from Mirkwood, and his retreat was but a feint. Ten years after the attack, Sauron declared himself openly in Mordor in [2951](../T/Third_Age_2951.md) and rebuilt [Barad-dûr](../B/Barad-dûr.md). The White Council met for one last time in [2953](../T/Third_Age_2953.md) to debate about the [Rings of Power](../R/Rings_of_Power.md). Saruman quieted his peers, claiming to have knowledge that the One Ring was lost in the [Belegaer](../B/Belegaer.md). After their meeting, Saruman, jealous and afraid of Gandalf, set spies to watch all his movements; this would affect the peaceful Hobbits, as from this, Saruman discovered their existence and noted Gandalf's interest in the Shire. As a result, he started sending agents to [Bree](../B/Bree.md) and the [Southfarthing](../S/Southfarthing.md).<sup id="cite_ref-ThirdAge_10-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ThirdAge-10">[10]</a></sup>

[Gollum](Gollum.md) had come to Gandalf's knowledge, having left his hideout. He was looking for the thief of his ring: Bilbo. The [Elves of Mirkwood](../E/Elves_of_Mirkwood.md) had informed Gandalf that Gollum was traversing the forest. Then, coinciding with Sauron's declaration, Gollum apparently turned towards [Mordor](../M/Mordor.md) and was lost. Gandalf neglected the matter because he "had much else to think of at that time" and still trusted Saruman's knowledge. Later, Gandalf would regret this.<sup id="cite_ref-shadow_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-shadow-26">[26]</a></sup>

Meanwhile, Sauron began reassembling his forces for the final blow against the West. [Easterlings](../E/Easterlings.md) from [Khand](../K/Khand.md) and beyond the [Sea of Rhûn](../S/Sea_of_Rhûn.md), joined by men from [Harad](../H/Harad.md) reinforced his stronghold in [Mordor](../M/Mordor.md); Orcs, trolls, and other foul beasts were multiplying while his minions were searching the Anduin for any sign of his precious One Ring.

Gandalf met [Aragorn](../A/Aragorn.md), the hidden [Heir of Isildur](../H/Heir_of_Isildur.md) in [2956](../T/Third_Age_2956.md), and soon became friends with him.<sup id="cite_ref-ThirdAge_10-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ThirdAge-10">[10]</a></sup> From that point on, Aragorn and Gandalf often worked together towards a common end - the defeat of Sauron.

He visited [Minas Tirith](../M/Minas_Tirith.md) and was welcomed by [Faramir](../F/Faramir.md), the son of the [Ruling Steward](../R/Ruling_Steward.md) [Denethor](../D/Denethor.md), and taught him from his wisdom. This displeased Denethor, who didn't trust Gandalf.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">[27]</a></sup>

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/M/Michele_Mantoani_-_Gandalf.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/c/c8/Michele_Mantoani_-_Gandalf.jpg/300px-Michele_Mantoani_-_Gandalf.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="300" height="389" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/c/c8/Michele_Mantoani_-_Gandalf.jpg/450px-Michele_Mantoani_-_Gandalf.jpg 1.5x, https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/c/c8/Michele_Mantoani_-_Gandalf.jpg/600px-Michele_Mantoani_-_Gandalf.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1920" data-file-height="2489"/></a><figcaption><i>Gandalf</i> by <a href="../../category/I/Images_by_Michele_Mantoani.md" title="Category:Images by Michele Mantoani">Michele Mantoani</a></figcaption></figure>

During this period Gandalf visited the Shire frequently, especially his friend Bilbo Baggins, and his younger cousin, [Frodo Baggins](../F/Frodo_Baggins.md). He noted Bilbo's unusual youthfulness, despite his advancing age; the suspicious "[magic ring](../T/The_One_Ring.md)" that Bilbo had acquired during his adventure began to weigh on his mind. Gandalf recalled the deceit Bilbo used in originally claiming it for his own — Bilbo had later admitted to stealing it from [Gollum](Gollum.md). Gandalf could see that Bilbo was very preoccupied with the ring. Such un-hobbitlike behaviour aroused his suspicions. In [3001](../T/Third_Age_3001.md) he organised [Bilbo's Farewell Party](../B/Bilbo's_Farewell_Party.md), and before leaving the Shire, he convinced Bilbo to leave the Ring for Frodo. He then emphatically warned Frodo not to use it; Gandalf had begun to suspect that the "magic ring" was indeed a [Ring of Power](../R/Ring_of_Power.md).<sup id="cite_ref-party_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-party-28">[28]</a></sup> Around that time, Gandalf became unpopular in the Shire; he was blamed for Bilbo's disappearance, and was even accused of plotting with Frodo to get a hold on Bilbo's wealth.<sup id="cite_ref-shadow_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-shadow-26">[26]</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-party_28-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-party-28">[28]</a></sup>

Gandalf was keen to find out more about Bilbo's ring, so he left the Shire,<sup id="cite_ref-party_28-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-party-28">[28]</a></sup> and resumed his search for Gollum. In this, he sought the help of [Aragorn](../A/Aragorn.md). The [Rangers of the North](../R/Rangers_of_the_North.md) were put to guard the Shire more closely.<sup id="cite_ref-ThirdAge_10-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ThirdAge-10">[10]</a></sup> Gandalf showed up only [three years later](../T/Third_Age_3004.md) to check on Frodo and the Shire, and continued for the next four years always turning up unexpectedly and disappearing before dawn. In [3009](../T/Third_Age_3009.md) he renewed the hunt for [Gollum](Gollum.md) with Aragorn, searching in the [vales of Anduin](../V/Vales_of_Anduin.md), [Mirkwood](../M/Mirkwood.md), [Rhovanion](../R/Rhovanion.md), and even to the edges of Mordor. The search lasted eight years, for Gandalf did not know that Gollum had been captured by Sauron.<sup id="cite_ref-ThirdAge_10-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ThirdAge-10">[10]</a></sup> Gandalf regretted ignoring Gollum for so many years, as his trail had become cold, resulting in many dark and dangerous days for Gandalf.<sup id="cite_ref-shadow_26-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-shadow-26">[26]</a></sup>

Studying the records in [Minas Tirith](../M/Minas_Tirith.md), Gandalf found the [Scroll of Isildur](../S/Scroll_of_Isildur.md) in [3017](../T/Third_Age_3017.md), and from this, pieced together the missing history of the One Ring. On his way back to the Shire, he got word from the [Galadhrim](Galadhrim.md) that Aragorn had finally captured Gollum<sup id="cite_ref-ThirdAge_10-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ThirdAge-10">[10]</a></sup> and so he went to [Mirkwood](../M/Mirkwood.md) to meet him. For days he interrogated him in order to verify what he already knew. A great fear came over him when he learned that Gollum had been to [Barad-dûr](../B/Barad-dûr.md). Sauron had tortured Gollum and learned of the "magic ring", as well as the names "Shire" and "Baggins". The effects that Gandalf had seen on Gollum and Bilbo convinced him that Bilbo's Ring was not simply one of the [Rings of Power](../R/Rings_of_Power.md): it was the *[One Ruling Ring](../T/The_One_Ring.md)* of Sauron. Gandalf then returned in haste to the Shire.<sup id="cite_ref-shadow_26-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-shadow-26">[26]</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-council_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-council-29">[29]</a></sup>

#### War of the Ring {#War_of_the_Ring}

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/A/Alan_Lee_-_The_Shadow_of_the_Past.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/d/d5/Alan_Lee_-_The_Shadow_of_the_Past.jpg/300px-Alan_Lee_-_The_Shadow_of_the_Past.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="300" height="491" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/d/d5/Alan_Lee_-_The_Shadow_of_the_Past.jpg/450px-Alan_Lee_-_The_Shadow_of_the_Past.jpg 1.5x, https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/d/d5/Alan_Lee_-_The_Shadow_of_the_Past.jpg/600px-Alan_Lee_-_The_Shadow_of_the_Past.jpg 2x" data-file-width="921" data-file-height="1507"/></a><figcaption><i>The Shadow of the Past</i> by <a href="../A/Alan_Lee.md" title="Alan Lee">Alan Lee</a></figcaption></figure>

Gandalf returned to the Shire on [12 April](../_/12_April.md) [T.A.](../T/Third_Age.md) [3018](../T/Third_Age_3018.md) and proved to Frodo that Bilbo's ring was the ancient [One Ring](../O/One_Ring.md) which the Dark Power of [Mordor](../M/Mordor.md) was looking for. He told Frodo that he gathered much of this information by interrogating the creature [Gollum](Gollum.md). Frodo exclaimed that Gollum should have been killed, however Gandalf speculated that perhaps Gollum would have a part to play before the end, and in any case, it was not for Frodo to decide who should live and who should die.<sup id="cite_ref-shadow_26-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-shadow-26">[26]</a></sup>

Gandalf suggested that he should leave the Shire soon and take the Ring to [Rivendell](../R/Rivendell.md). Frodo decided to leave on his next birthday, pretending to be moving to [Buckland](../B/Buckland.md), to which Gandalf agreed. The wizard stayed in the Shire for over two months,<sup id="cite_ref-three_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-three-30">[30]</a></sup> during which time, on [1 May](../_/1_May.md) he went to [Sarn Ford](../S/Sarn_Ford.md) and met again Aragorn, informing him of Frodo's plans.<sup id="cite_ref-FR|Strider_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FR|Strider-31">[31]</a></sup> He had a sense of foreboding and heard [disturbing news about war](../S/Sauron's_attack_on_Osgiliath.md) in [Gondor](Gondor.md) and a [Black Shadow](../B/Black_Shadow.md).<sup id="cite_ref-council_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-council-29">[29]</a></sup> He decided to leave for the South and left Frodo, promising to return for his birthday-farewell party in autumn, so that they would go to Rivendell together.<sup id="cite_ref-three_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-three-30">[30]</a></sup>

He wandered around [Eriador](../E/Eriador.md) hearing news from the refugees who had a fear they could not speak about, until he met [Radagast](../R/Radagast.md) who brought a message from [Saruman](../S/Saruman.md) that he must seek him at once, and a warning that the [Ringwraiths](../R/Ringwraiths.md) were looking for the Shire.<sup id="cite_ref-council_29-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-council-29">[29]</a></sup> Gandalf went to [the Prancing Pony](../T/The_Prancing_Pony.md) at [Bree](../B/Bree.md). Believing that he would not be able to return to Frodo in time, he wrote [a letter](Gandalf's_letter.md),<sup id="cite_ref-council_29-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-council-29">[29]</a></sup> urging him to move as soon as possible for Rivendell, and seek someone named "Strider", whose real name was Aragorn, with a [riddle](../R/Riddle_of_Strider.md) to identify him; Gandalf would then try to catch up with them when he could. He instructed [Barliman Butterbur](../B/Barliman_Butterbur.md) to send the letter to [Hobbiton](../H/Hobbiton.md) and to expect a Mr. [Baggins](../B/Baggins.md) to arrive at the inn under the name of "[Mr. Underhill](../M/Mr._Underhill.md)". He left the inn, but Barliman would forget to send the letter.<sup id="cite_ref-FR|Strider_31-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FR|Strider-31">[31]</a></sup>

##### Treachery of Saruman {#Treachery_of_Saruman}

<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/T/Ted_Nasmith_-_Gandalf_Escapes_Upon_Gwaihir.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/e/ea/Ted_Nasmith_-_Gandalf_Escapes_Upon_Gwaihir.jpg/300px-Ted_Nasmith_-_Gandalf_Escapes_Upon_Gwaihir.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="300" height="408" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/e/ea/Ted_Nasmith_-_Gandalf_Escapes_Upon_Gwaihir.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="331" data-file-height="450"/></a><figcaption><i>Gandalf Escapes Upon Gwaihir</i> by <a href="../T/Ted_Nasmith.md" title="Ted Nasmith">Ted Nasmith</a></figcaption></figure>

Soon thereafter, Gandalf arrived at Isengard. At their meeting, Saruman at last revealed his desire for the One Ring.<sup id="cite_ref-council_29-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-council-29">[29]</a></sup> He offered to his "*old friend and helper*" that they take the Ring for themselves and seize power from Sauron. Gandalf rejected this with horror, and was imprisoned by Saruman on the pinnacle of [Orthanc](../O/Orthanc.md). [Gwaihir](Gwaihir.md), chief of the Eagles, soon arrived and helped Gandalf escape.<sup id="cite_ref-council_29-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-council-29">[29]</a></sup> Gandalf knew he needed to return quickly to the Shire, as Frodo (and the Ring) were in grave danger from both Sauron's Nazgûl and Saruman's treacherous desire for the Ring.

Gandalf hurriedly went to [Rohan](../R/Rohan.md), desiring to find a strong steed; there he obtained [Shadowfax](../S/Shadowfax.md) from King [Théoden](../T/Théoden.md), who later resented the gift.<sup id="cite_ref-council_29-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-council-29">[29]</a></sup> This lord of horses and Gandalf forged a special bond, and Gandalf made quick use of Shadowfax's incredible strength and endurance.

Gandalf sped to the Shire. Fortunately, Frodo had already left the Shire without waiting for Gandalf,<sup id="cite_ref-council_29-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-council-29">[29]</a></sup> and was seeking [Rivendell](../R/Rivendell.md). Upon his arrival, Gandalf learned that the Nazgûl, arrayed as [Black Riders](../N/Nazgûl.md), had been searching the area. Dismayed, he set out for Bree; Barliman apologised to Gandalf for forgetting to send the letter, and was worried that the hobbits had left with Strider, the suspicious [Ranger](../R/Rangers_of_the_North.md). But for Gandalf, this was a hope which far exceeded his expectations. After congratulating a puzzled Barliman, Gandalf slept the night at the inn and in the morning left for [Weathertop](../W/Weathertop.md), a high point in the region, to observe the surrounding area.<sup id="cite_ref-council_29-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-council-29">[29]</a></sup>

<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/E/Eric_Velhagen_-_Encounter_at_Amon_Sûl.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/0/05/Eric_Velhagen_-_Encounter_at_Amon_S%C3%BBl.jpg/250px-Eric_Velhagen_-_Encounter_at_Amon_S%C3%BBl.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="250" height="157" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/0/05/Eric_Velhagen_-_Encounter_at_Amon_S%C3%BBl.jpg/375px-Eric_Velhagen_-_Encounter_at_Amon_S%C3%BBl.jpg 1.5x, https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/0/05/Eric_Velhagen_-_Encounter_at_Amon_S%C3%BBl.jpg/500px-Eric_Velhagen_-_Encounter_at_Amon_S%C3%BBl.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1800" data-file-height="1133"/></a><figcaption><i>Encounter at <a href="../W/Weathertop.md" title="Weathertop">Amon Sûl</a></i> by <a href="../E/Eric_Velhagen.md" title="Eric Velhagen">Eric Velhagen</a></figcaption></figure>

There he was assaulted at night by the Nazgûl, but drove them off after a great battle of light and flame.<sup id="cite_ref-B3_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-B3-32">[32]</a></sup> These phenomena were seen by Aragorn and the hobbits from afar, although they were unaware that Gandalf was involved.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">[33]</a></sup> Before leaving Weathertop, he marked some stones with the [certh](../C/Cirth.md) G for them to find. The next day he escaped to the north, followed by four Riders.<sup id="cite_ref-council_29-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-council-29">[29]</a></sup>

He then went directly to Rivendell, arriving a few days sooner than [Aragorn](../A/Aragorn.md).<sup id="cite_ref-B3_32-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-B3-32">[32]</a></sup> But when they arrived, Gandalf and Elrond joined their powers to cause a flood in [Bruinen](../B/Bruinen.md) to drown the Riders, allowing Frodo some time to reach Rivendell.<sup id="cite_ref-council_29-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-council-29">[29]</a></sup> Then they welcomed Glorfindel, Aragorn and the hobbits upon their arrival. Frodo was sick with a [Morgul-wound](../M/Morgul-wound.md), but was still in possession of the Ring.

##### Fellowship of the Ring {#Fellowship_of_the_Ring}

Elrond called a [Council](../C/Council_of_Elrond.md) after Frodo was healed to consider the momentous decision of what would be done with the Ring. By chance, representatives of most of the free peoples were in Rivendell for various reasons. Elrond and Gandalf advised that the Ring should be destroyed in the fires of [Orodruin](../O/Orodruin.md), where it was made. Others dissented or objected, but they eventually submitted to Gandalf's plan. Ultimately, Elrond appointed the [Fellowship of the Ring](../F/Fellowship_of_the_Ring.md) as nine walkers, numerically set against Sauron's nine Nazgûl. The relatively small number reflected the realisation by Elrond and the other council members that the quest of Mount Doom would not rely upon strength of arms, but on stealth and good fortune. Gandalf was chosen to lead the company, which included [Aragorn](../A/Aragorn.md), [Boromir](../B/Boromir.md), [Legolas](../L/Legolas.md) the Elf, [Gimli](Gimli.md) the Dwarf, and the hobbits Frodo Baggins, who was named the [Ring-bearer](../R/Ring-bearer.md), and [Samwise Gamgee](../S/Samwise_Gamgee.md), [Peregrin Took](../P/Peregrin_Took.md), and [Meriadoc Brandybuck](../M/Meriadoc_Brandybuck.md).<sup id="cite_ref-council_29-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-council-29">[29]</a></sup>

Several obstacles stood in the company's way. The vast [Misty Mountains](../M/Misty_Mountains.md) had to be crossed, for Gandalf was determined not to lead the company near [Isengard](../I/Isengard.md). Gandalf decided to take a southern route to the [Redhorn Pass](../R/Redhorn_Pass.md) and there to cross the [Misty Mountains](../M/Misty_Mountains.md) near [Caradhras](../C/Caradhras.md), thereby traversing the mountain range and avoiding Isengard.<sup id="cite_ref-The_Ring_Goes_South,_p._283_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Ring_Goes_South,_p._283-34">[34]</a></sup> When this attempt failed due to a terrible storm, he decided to take the Fellowship through the ruins of Moria, where the remains of the great Dwarven city of [Khazad-dûm](../M/Moria.md) were now a labyrinth of tunnels under the mountains. Others in the company were loath to enter the maze, as it was now the lair of orcs and something known only as "Durin's Bane".

At the [Doors of Durin](../D/Doors_of_Durin.md) on the west side of the mountains, Gandalf, after some delay, spoke the password and led the company into the dark. Having been in Moria on an earlier perilous errand, he was somewhat familiar with the underground passages. Eventually the party came to the [Chamber of Mazarbul](../C/Chamber_of_Mazarbul.md), where Gandalf read the [Book of Records](../B/Book_of_Mazarbul.md), which revealed the fate of [Balin](../B/Balin.md), the leader of an ill-fated attempt to re-colonise Moria. Soon after, the party was attacked by orcs, and forced to flee the chamber. By then, Gandalf was well aware of their location and he led the party quickly towards the eastern exit.

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/J/John_Howe_-_Gandalf_Falls_with_the_Balrog.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/f/f9/John_Howe_-_Gandalf_Falls_with_the_Balrog.jpg/300px-John_Howe_-_Gandalf_Falls_with_the_Balrog.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="300" height="476" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/f/f9/John_Howe_-_Gandalf_Falls_with_the_Balrog.jpg/450px-John_Howe_-_Gandalf_Falls_with_the_Balrog.jpg 1.5x, https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/f/f9/John_Howe_-_Gandalf_Falls_with_the_Balrog.jpg 2x" data-file-width="534" data-file-height="848"/></a><figcaption><i>Gandalf Falls with the Balrog</i> by <a href="../J/John_Howe.md" title="John Howe">John Howe</a></figcaption></figure>

However, [Durin's Bane](../D/Durin's_Bane.md) caught up to the group at the [Bridge of Khazad-dûm](../B/Bridge_of_Khazad-dûm.md). Gandalf and Legolas immediately realised what it was: a [Balrog](../B/Balrogs.md) of [Morgoth](../M/Morgoth.md). Gandalf faced the demon and broke the bridge upon which they both stood, leaving the beast to fall into a seemingly bottomless chasm. But the Balrog's whip lashed out, and grasped Gandalf by the knees, causing him to fall into the pit. While falling, Gandalf shouted "Fly, you fools" and vanished into the abyss.<sup id="cite_ref-bridge_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bridge-35">[35]</a></sup>

Yet Gandalf did not die; he and the Balrog fought long in the bowels and deep places of [Arda](../A/Arda.md). After restless pursuit over the course of eight days, Gandalf and the beast finally came to the summit of the [Endless Stair](../E/Endless_Stair.md) upon the peak of [Celebdil](../C/Celebdil.md). There, he [fought the demon](../B/Battle_of_the_Peak.md) for two days and nights. The Balrog had burst into flame anew when it exited the stairs; ice, wind, and smoke swirled about them as they duelled. Gandalf used his last measure of strength to slay the Balrog, throwing him down the mountainside in ruin. Gandalf's spirit then left his body, having sacrificed himself to save the Fellowship.

##### Gandalf the White {#Gandalf_the_White}

But Gandalf's spirit did not depart Middle-earth forever. As the only one of the five Istari to stay true to his errand, Olórin was sent back to mortal lands by [Eru](../I/Ilúvatar.md), and he became Gandalf once again. Yet, as he was now the sole emissary of the Valar to Middle-earth, he was granted the power to "reveal" more of his inner [Maiar](../M/Maiar.md) strength.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">[36]</a></sup> This naked power that lay within him was seldom used during the remainder of his time in Middle-earth, as his mission was essentially the same: to support and succour those who opposed Sauron. Nevertheless, when Gandalf's wrath was kindled his "unveiled" strength was such that few of Sauron's servants could withstand him.

<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/T/Ted_Nasmith_-_Gandalf_Returns.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/7/7e/Ted_Nasmith_-_Gandalf_Returns.jpg/300px-Ted_Nasmith_-_Gandalf_Returns.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="300" height="286" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/7/7e/Ted_Nasmith_-_Gandalf_Returns.jpg/450px-Ted_Nasmith_-_Gandalf_Returns.jpg 1.5x, https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/7/7e/Ted_Nasmith_-_Gandalf_Returns.jpg/600px-Ted_Nasmith_-_Gandalf_Returns.jpg 2x" data-file-width="960" data-file-height="915"/></a><figcaption><i>Gandalf Returns</i> by <a href="../T/Ted_Nasmith.md" title="Ted Nasmith">Ted Nasmith</a></figcaption></figure>

As he lay on the mountaintop, the great eagle [Gwaihir](Gwaihir.md) came up and bore him to [Lothlórien](../L/Lothlórien.md), where he was clothed and replenished, and given a new staff by [Galadriel](Galadriel.md). Gandalf soon learned that Frodo had left the Fellowship and was travelling to Mount Doom. As Frodo was beyond his assistance now, Gandalf promptly went south to [Fangorn Forest](../F/Fangorn_Forest.md), where he met the [Three Hunters](../T/Three_Hunters.md): [Aragorn](../A/Aragorn.md), [Legolas](../L/Legolas.md), and [Gimli](Gimli.md), and gave them messages from Galadriel. From them, he learned that Sam went with Frodo, and he was pleased with this.<sup id="cite_ref-TT_White_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TT_White-37">[37]</a></sup><sup class="references" style="margin: 0;line-height: 0.4em; padding-top: 0px !important; font-size: 70%;">:496</sup> Then he called forth [Shadowfax](../S/Shadowfax.md), and rode with them to [Edoras](../E/Edoras.md). There he found that Saruman's spy [Gríma Wormtongue](Gríma.md) had deceived King [Théoden](../T/Théoden.md) into hopeless impotence. Gandalf quickly deposed Wormtongue and encouraged Théoden to ride west to war against [Saruman](../S/Saruman.md). By now, Gandalf was keenly aware that the great war to end the age was beginning; if Saruman conquered Rohan, then Gondor would be alone with enemies on all sides.

At Gandalf's encouragement, King Théoden went west to Helm's Deep where he was quickly besieged; Gandalf then searched for [Erkenbrand](../E/Erkenbrand.md) and the forces of the [Westfold](../W/Westfold.md), which he later found and led to the Deep, thus breaking the siege. Meanwhile, the [Ents](../E/Ents.md), along with the hobbits Merry and Pippin, moved against Saruman and sent [Huorns](../H/Huorns.md) against the orcs, resulting in the utter ruin of the outer walls of [Isengard](../I/Isengard.md) and the complete annihilation of Saruman's orcs. After the battle, Gandalf went to [Orthanc](../O/Orthanc.md) with Théoden, Aragorn, and a small group. There Saruman rejected Gandalf's offer of forgiveness with contempt. Gandalf then broke Saruman's staff and cast him from the Order and the Council.<sup id="cite_ref-TT_Voice_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TT_Voice-38">[38]</a></sup><sup class="references" style="margin: 0;line-height: 0.4em; padding-top: 0px !important; font-size: 70%;">:583</sup> Gandalf imposed a strict watch on Isengard by the Ents and then advised King Théoden to ride to Gondor's defence as soon as possible. The wizard's mind had already turned to Gondor and the coming climactic battle in the east.

##### Siege of Minas Tirith {#Siege_of_Minas_Tirith}

<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/D/Donato_Giancola_-_The_White_Rider.jpeg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/7/75/Donato_Giancola_-_The_White_Rider.jpeg/200px-Donato_Giancola_-_The_White_Rider.jpeg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="200" height="120" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/7/75/Donato_Giancola_-_The_White_Rider.jpeg/300px-Donato_Giancola_-_The_White_Rider.jpeg 1.5x, https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/7/75/Donato_Giancola_-_The_White_Rider.jpeg/400px-Donato_Giancola_-_The_White_Rider.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="478"/></a><figcaption><i>The White Rider</i> by <a href="../D/Donato_Giancola.md" title="Donato Giancola">Donato Giancola</a></figcaption></figure>

As a 'reward' for Pippin, who had foolishly gazed into a [palantír](../P/Palantíri.md), Gandalf took the Hobbit with him to [Minas Tirith](../M/Minas_Tirith.md), the last bastion of the west. Soon after arriving, Gandalf confronted [Denethor II](../D/Denethor.md), the [Ruling Steward](../R/Ruling_Steward.md), and learned that he was near despair over the death of his eldest son, [Boromir](../B/Boromir.md). Pippin entered the Steward's service in payment of the debt that he and Merry owed: the death of Boromir. Ostensibly they were allies, but the Steward treated him with disrespect and suspicion. When [Faramir](../F/Faramir.md), the Steward's younger son, returned from [Osgiliath](../O/Osgiliath.md) and was attacked by Nazgûl, Gandalf upon Shadowfax drove them away by revealing the power within him. Later, Faramir told him that Frodo and Sam were still alive and headed towards Mordor.<sup id="cite_ref-RK_Siege_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RK_Siege-39">[39]</a></sup><sup class="references" style="margin: 0;line-height: 0.4em; padding-top: 0px !important; font-size: 70%;">:812</sup>

The city was soon besieged by a vast force from Mordor, led by the [Witch-king](../W/Witch-king.md). An ill-advised counterattack resulted in Faramir receiving a wound from a poisoned dart; he lay near death inside the Tower. Still, Gandalf encouraged the men of Minas Tirith to have hope, and dispelled the fear of the Ringwraiths by his very presence. But Sauron's catapults hurled flaming bolts upon the city; soon the first circle of the city burned unchecked. Denethor then lost all heart as the city burned and his only remaining son hovered near death. He abandoned his leadership of the city, and Gandalf then took it upon himself to direct the defence of the city. When the gigantic battering ram [Grond](<Grond_(battering_ram).md>) destroyed the ancient entrance to the city, Gandalf placed himself alone at the ruined gateway. The Witch-king then appeared in the midst of the blasted gate upon a black horse and threatened Gandalf with death; but Gandalf did not move — seated upon Shadowfax he defied the mightiest of Sauron's minions. However, the stand-off ended inconclusively, as the morning arrived along with the host of the Rohirrim. Hearing the horns of the [Riders of Rohan](../R/Riders_of_Rohan.md), the Witch-king departed.

However, Gandalf did not pursue his foe, for Pippin brought him news that Denethor was about to burn himself and his son Faramir to death on a pyre like the heathen kings of old. Gandalf rushed to stop this, and was able to save Faramir, but not Denethor, whose despair and grief had overcome his mind. Gandalf then learned how it was that Denethor's will had been broken: the Steward clutched a palantír in his hands as he burned.

Nevertheless, and against all hope, the siege was broken. [Éowyn](../_/Éowyn.md) of Rohan and Merry defeated the Witch-king, whose last wail was heard by many as he was reduced to impotence. Soon after, Aragorn arrived with a large host of men from the southern fiefs upon a captured pirate fleet from Umbar. The forces of the men of the west then utterly defeated Sauron's attack against Minas Tirith, relieving the city and killing virtually all of the invaders. Gandalf's carefully laid plans and words of wisdom, along with acts of heroism not seen since the [Elder Days](../E/Elder_Days.md), had defeated Sauron's first move.

##### Last battle {#Last_battle}

But the attack against Minas Tirith was only a part of Sauron's plan to devastate the West and make himself the ruler of Middle-earth. Other armies moved in the north against [Erebor](../L/Lonely_Mountain.md) and the Kingdom of Thranduil, as well as against Lothlórien and other points along the [Anduin](../A/Anduin.md). Still commanding vast armies of orcs and men, the Dark Lord soon would move against Gondor again; the remnants of the Edain and their allies had little hope against his almost limitless resources.

In Minas Tirith, Gandalf was selected by Aragorn, [Imrahil](../I/Imrahil.md), and [Éomer](../_/Éomer.md) (the remaining lords of the west) to be their leader in the coming final battles. This would be the culmination of Gandalf's efforts in Middle-earth. Fully aware that the West would stand or fall on the outcome of Frodo's mission, he advised the lords to drive north to the [Morannon](../M/Morannon.md), thereby drawing Sauron's eye away from Frodo's likely location. This plan would likely result in a catastrophic loss for the outnumbered army, but it gave Frodo a chance to achieve the quest of Mount Doom.

Led by Gandalf and Aragorn, the army of the West crossed the Anduin and marched north, pausing occasionally to announce their coming, and to dispatch small numbers of men to lesser tasks. Upon arriving at the Black Gate, the forces halted and prepared for battle. As they ordered their companies, the foul [Mouth of Sauron](../M/Mouth_of_Sauron.md) rode forth to parley with them; he revealed Frodo's [Mithril-coat](../M/Mithril-coat.md) and [Barrow-blade](../B/Barrow-blade.md) and implied that their owner was captured and tortured. The emissary of Sauron then proposed that the forces of the west surrender; Gandalf however was undaunted, and, seizing his friend's belongings, rejected Sauron's offer. In shock, the Mouth of Sauron turned back towards the Black Gate, which slowly opened to reveal a vast army of orcs and trolls advancing on the lords of the west. Sauron's trap was sprung.

Yet Sauron himself became the victim of Gandalf's trap. Unbeknownst to all, Frodo and Sam had succeeded in scaling Mount Doom. Even as the Battle of Morannon began, Frodo stood at the [Crack of Doom](../C/Crack_of_Doom.md). But the power and lure of the Ring finally overcame his will and he placed the ring upon his finger, claiming it as his own. Immediately the Nazgûl were summoned by their lord, as he in terror realised his blunder: his enemies intended to destroy his Ring.

<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/T/Ted_Nasmith_-_The_Shadow_of_Sauron.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/d/d1/Ted_Nasmith_-_The_Shadow_of_Sauron.jpg/200px-Ted_Nasmith_-_The_Shadow_of_Sauron.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="200" height="152" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/d/d1/Ted_Nasmith_-_The_Shadow_of_Sauron.jpg/300px-Ted_Nasmith_-_The_Shadow_of_Sauron.jpg 1.5x, https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/d/d1/Ted_Nasmith_-_The_Shadow_of_Sauron.jpg/400px-Ted_Nasmith_-_The_Shadow_of_Sauron.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1221" data-file-height="929"/></a><figcaption><i>The Shadow of Sauron</i> by <a href="../T/Ted_Nasmith.md" title="Ted Nasmith">Ted Nasmith</a></figcaption></figure>

But Gandalf's foresight proved accurate again as the creature Gollum, who had been doggedly following the Ring-bearer, seized the Ring from Frodo, and, while celebrating his reunion with "his precious," unwittingly fell into the fires of Orodruin. The ring was unmade as the fiery mountain erupted. The tower of [Barad-dûr](../B/Barad-dûr.md) and the [Towers of the Teeth](../T/Towers_of_the_Teeth.md) began to collapse, their foundations crumbling. The Ring-wraiths burned out like shooting stars, and Sauron was reduced to a mere shadow of malice, never to torment the world again.

With Sauron gone, his forces scattered; the Men of the West now set upon them with fury. Gandalf announced the success of the [Ring-bearer](../R/Ring-bearer.md) and the end of Sauron; the quest had been fulfilled. Seeing that victory was achieved, Gandalf then mounted [Gwaihir](Gwaihir.md) the Eagle for the third time, and set out for Mount Doom in search of Frodo and Sam. To his great relief, the two were found on the slopes of Orodruin, clinging to life amid the volcanic eruptions.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">[40]</a></sup><sup class="references" style="margin: 0;line-height: 0.4em; padding-top: 0px !important; font-size: 70%;">:948–51</sup> The great quest was over.

#### End of the Order {#End_of_the_Order}

In Minas Tirith, Gandalf and the remaining members of the Fellowship reunited. At the coronation of King [Elessar](../A/Aragorn.md), Gandalf, at Aragorn's request, set the crown upon the King’s head, and declared "*Now come the days of the King, and may they be blessed while the thrones of the [Valar](../V/Valar.md) endure!*". Thus Gandalf ushered in the new age of men.

After the coronation and wedding of Aragorn to [Arwen](../A/Arwen.md), Gandalf left with the rest of the remaining Fellowship on the journey home. For Gandalf, it was his last long journey in Middle-earth. His errand to Arda had been fulfilled; Sauron had been defeated. He said farewell to his friends one by one until at last only the four Hobbits remained at his side. At the borders of the Shire he, too, turned away. He left the Hobbits to settle the affairs of the Shire themselves, for the shattered pieces of evil still remaining in the world were no longer his concern, and went to talk to [Tom Bombadil](../T/Tom_Bombadil.md).<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">[41]</a></sup><sup class="references" style="margin: 0;line-height: 0.4em; padding-top: 0px !important; font-size: 70%;">:996</sup>

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/T/The_Brothers_Hildebrandt_-_At_the_Grey_Havens.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/e/e3/The_Brothers_Hildebrandt_-_At_the_Grey_Havens.jpg/300px-The_Brothers_Hildebrandt_-_At_the_Grey_Havens.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="300" height="149" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/e/e3/The_Brothers_Hildebrandt_-_At_the_Grey_Havens.jpg/450px-The_Brothers_Hildebrandt_-_At_the_Grey_Havens.jpg 1.5x, https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/e/e3/The_Brothers_Hildebrandt_-_At_the_Grey_Havens.jpg/600px-The_Brothers_Hildebrandt_-_At_the_Grey_Havens.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1615" data-file-height="800"/></a><figcaption><i>At the Gray Havens</i> by <a href="../T/The_Brothers_Hildebrandt.md" title="The Brothers Hildebrandt">The Brothers Hildebrandt</a></figcaption></figure>

What Gandalf did during the next two years is unknown; it is possible that his "*long talk*" with Bombadil was just that. At any rate, on [29 September](../_/29_September.md) [3021](../T/Third_Age_3021.md), he met [Frodo](../F/Frodo_Baggins.md) at [Mithlond](../M/Mithlond.md), ready to take the [White Ship](../W/White_Ship.md) over the [sea](../B/Belegaer.md) to [Aman](../A/Aman.md). He wore [Narya](../N/Narya.md) openly on his finger, and Shadowfax was beside him (perhaps even to take ship with him). His mission was over, and his homecoming after more than 2000 years was nigh. He bade farewell to [Samwise](../S/Samwise_Gamgee.md), [Merry](../M/Meriadoc_Brandybuck.md), and [Pippin](../P/Peregrin_Took.md) (the later two of whom he had forewarned of the passage), then boarded the ship beside Frodo, Bilbo, Elrond, and Galadriel. The ship passed west upon the sea,<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">[42]</a></sup> and then took the hidden straight path to [Valinor](../V/Valinor.md). Gandalf became Olórin once more. There, presumably, he dwells still in the gardens of [Irmo](../I/Irmo.md). Olórin, the wisest of the Maiar and the sole Istar to remain true to his mission, had successfully kindled the hearts of the [free peoples](../F/Free_peoples~19934.md) of Middle-earth to overcome the evil of their time.

## Characteristics {#Characteristics}

### Personality {#Personality}

<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/R/Roger_Garland_-_Gandalf.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/8/81/Roger_Garland_-_Gandalf.jpg/300px-Roger_Garland_-_Gandalf.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="300" height="319" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/8/81/Roger_Garland_-_Gandalf.jpg/450px-Roger_Garland_-_Gandalf.jpg 1.5x, https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/8/81/Roger_Garland_-_Gandalf.jpg/600px-Roger_Garland_-_Gandalf.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1174" data-file-height="1250"/></a><figcaption>The Grey Pilgrim relaxing. Art by <a href="../R/Roger_Garland.md" title="Roger Garland">Roger Garland</a></figcaption></figure>

<blockquote class="tg-quote noexcerpt">
<p>A deadly sword, a healing hand,
a back that bent beneath his load;
a trumpet-voice, a burning brand,
a weary pilgrim on the road.
</p><p>A lord of wisdom throned he sat,
swift in anger, quick to laugh;
an old man in a battered hat
who leaned upon a thorny staff.
</p>
<p class="tg-quote-footer"><cite><i><a href="../T/The_Fellowship_of_the_Ring.md" title="The Fellowship of the Ring">The Fellowship of the Ring</a></i>, &#34;<a href="../T/The_Mirror_of_Galadriel.md" title="The Mirror of Galadriel">The Mirror of Galadriel</a>&#34;</cite></p>
</blockquote>

Gandalf is often described in *[The Lord of the Rings](../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings.md)* as quick to anger, and equally quick to laugh.<sup id="cite_ref-Lorien_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Lorien-43">[43]</a></sup> He had great pity and patience, even extending to the servants of his enemies. He is said to have learned both from Nienna in Valinor.<sup id="cite_ref-vala_7-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vala-7">[7]</a></sup>

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/J/J.R.R._Tolkien_-_G_for_Gandalf.png.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/8/84/J.R.R._Tolkien_-_G_for_Gandalf.png" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="90" height="129" class="mw-file-element" data-file-width="90" data-file-height="129"/></a><figcaption>Gandalf&#39;s signature, the <a href="../C/Cirth.md" title="Cirth">rune</a> &#34;G&#34;.</figcaption></figure>

Keen observers of Gandalf often detected a veiled power, usually revealed in his eyes, which appeared deep and wise.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">[44]</a></sup><sup class="references" style="margin: 0;line-height: 0.4em; padding-top: 0px !important; font-size: 70%;">:757</sup> He was alternately affectionate and brusque; he often surprised others with his bluntness when time was of the essence. Gandalf consistently upbraided foolish behaviour, but also richly rewarded those who acted with good intentions.

Hobbits appealed to him more than to the other Wizards, and he often went to the Shire for respites from his errands. It may be that he was amused by their nature. It may also be because they were untouched by the great evils of the world, and were more in touch with nature than Men; perhaps their agrarian lifestyle appealed to Gandalf's innate spirit and reminded him of the gardens of Valinor.

[Círdan](../C/Círdan.md) the Shipwright divined in him "the greatest spirit and the wisest" out of all of the Wizards that came from the West, and so he entrusted the care of [Narya](../N/Narya.md), one of the [Three Rings](../T/Three_Rings.md) of the Elves, to Gandalf rather than to Saruman.<sup id="cite_ref-Istari_9-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Istari-9">[9]</a></sup>

### Appearance {#Appearance}

<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/J/John_Howe_-_Gandalf_the_Grey_02.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/7/77/John_Howe_-_Gandalf_the_Grey_02.jpg/200px-John_Howe_-_Gandalf_the_Grey_02.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="200" height="137" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/7/77/John_Howe_-_Gandalf_the_Grey_02.jpg/300px-John_Howe_-_Gandalf_the_Grey_02.jpg 1.5x, https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/7/77/John_Howe_-_Gandalf_the_Grey_02.jpg/400px-John_Howe_-_Gandalf_the_Grey_02.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1422" data-file-height="976"/></a><figcaption><i>Gandalf the Grey</i> by <a href="../J/John_Howe.md" title="John Howe">John Howe</a></figcaption></figure>

The first description of Gandalf is preserved in the initial pages of *The Hobbit*, written in the early 1930s. Gandalf's fame is alluded to even before his physical description ("Tales and adventures sprouted up all over the place wherever he went, in the most extraordinary fashion"), while the protagonist's ("unsuspecting Bilbo") impression is that of:

<blockquote class="tg-quote noexcerpt">
<p>...an old man with a staff. He had a tall pointed blue hat, a long grey cloak, a silver scarf over which a white beard hung down below his waist, and immense black boots.
</p>
<p class="tg-quote-footer"><cite><i><a href="../T/The_Hobbit.md" title="The Hobbit">The Hobbit</a></i>, &#34;<a href="../A/An_Unexpected_Party.md" title="An Unexpected Party">An Unexpected Party</a>&#34;</cite></p>
</blockquote>

Later, [Tolkien](../J/J.R.R._Tolkien.md) wrote:

<blockquote class="tg-quote noexcerpt">
<p>...a figure strongly built and with broad shoulder, though shorter than the average of men and now stooped with age, leaning on a thick rough-cut staff as he trudged along... Gandalf&#39;s hat was wide-brimmed [...] with a pointed conical crown, and it was <i>blue</i>; he wore a long <i>grey</i> cloak, but this would not reach much below his knees. It was of an elven silver-grey hue, though tarnished by wear - as is evident from the general use of grey in <a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings.md" title="The Lord of the Rings">the book</a>... But his colours were always white, silver-grey, and blue - except for the boots he wore when walking in the wild...Gandalf even bent must have been at least 5 ft. 6... Which would make him a short man even in modern England, especially with the reduction of a bent back.<sup id="cite_ref-MB_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MB-45">[45]</a></sup>
</p>
</blockquote>

Although some of the Wise knew his true nature, his "wizard-like" appearance caused many to mistake him for a simple conjurer. After his return, his "signature colour" changed from grey to white, for he had been sent back to replace the corrupt Saruman as the chief of the Wizards. In the book, he claimed that, in some ways, he now "was Saruman", or rather Saruman as he should have been.<sup id="cite_ref-TT_White_37-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TT_White-37">[37]</a></sup><sup class="references" style="margin: 0;line-height: 0.4em; padding-top: 0px !important; font-size: 70%;">:495</sup>

### Powers and abilities {#Powers_and_abilities}

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/R/Roger_Thomasson_-_Gandalf_the_Grey.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/6/66/Roger_Thomasson_-_Gandalf_the_Grey.jpg/300px-Roger_Thomasson_-_Gandalf_the_Grey.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="300" height="594" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/6/66/Roger_Thomasson_-_Gandalf_the_Grey.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="303" data-file-height="600"/></a><figcaption><i>Gandalf the Grey</i> by <a href="../R/Roger_Thomasson.md" title="Roger Thomasson">Roger Thomasson</a></figcaption></figure>

Gandalf demonstrated extensive knowledge of the land and an assortment of magical abilities from trivial to essential.

Gandalf was the bearer of [Narya](../N/Narya.md), the Ring of Fire, and described himself as the "servant of the [Secret Fire](../S/Secret_Fire.md)" and "wielder of the [flame of Anor](../F/Flame_of_Anor.md)".<sup id="cite_ref-bridge_35-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bridge-35">[35]</a></sup> Many of Gandalf's displays of power were related to fire in some way. It is not known, though, whether or to what extent his possession of Narya affected his abilities.

He created blinding flashes and other pyrotechnics to distract the [goblins](../O/Orcs.md) of the [Misty Mountains](../M/Misty_Mountains.md) during Thorin and Company's escape from [Goblin-town](Goblin-town.md). On the eastern slopes, he turned pine cones into flaming projectiles that threw hot sparks and started fires that would not easily go out.

When the Fellowship was attacked by [Wargs](../W/Wargs.md) in [Hollin](../E/Eregion.md), Gandalf spoke words of power to inflame the trees on the hillock where the company had camped. While crossing the Misty Mountains with the Fellowship, he was able to start fires under blizzard conditions. On the journey through Moria he created lights of varying intensity, magically secured doors, and broke the [Bridge of Khazad-dûm](../B/Bridge_of_Khazad-dûm.md). He fought and killed the [Balrog of Moria](../D/Durin's_Bane.md), although he did not himself survive the battle.<sup id="cite_ref-bridge_35-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bridge-35">[35]</a></sup>

Sent back to [Middle-earth](../M/Middle-earth.md) as Gandalf the White, he possessed greater charisma and a limited degree of clairvoyance, although he was unable to peer into the land of [Mordor](../M/Mordor.md) to see the progress of Frodo and [Sam](../S/Samwise_Gamgee.md). His power and authority had increased so that he could break [Saruman](../S/Saruman.md)'s staff with a spoken command, throw the treacherous wizard out of the order.<sup id="cite_ref-TT_Voice_38-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TT_Voice-38">[38]</a></sup><sup class="references" style="margin: 0;line-height: 0.4em; padding-top: 0px !important; font-size: 70%;">:583</sup> He also prevented Sauron's Eye from locating Frodo.<sup id="cite_ref-TT_White_37-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TT_White-37">[37]</a></sup>

He frequently used his powers for entertainment. He blew glowing smoke rings that moved around the room at his direction, and was known among Hobbits for his fireworks displays,<sup id="cite_ref-Erebor_14-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Erebor-14">[14]</a></sup> for example at [Bilbo's Farewell Party](../B/Bilbo's_Farewell_Party.md).<sup id="cite_ref-party_28-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-party-28">[28]</a></sup>

When angered or aroused for battle, he seemed to grow in height and assume a terrifying aspect.<sup id="cite_ref-party_28-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-party-28">[28]</a></sup><sup class="references" style="margin: 0;line-height: 0.4em; padding-top: 0px !important; font-size: 70%;">:34</sup>

Though not as keen as [Elves](../E/Elves.md), his eyesight was sharp enough to see in darkness, and into the [Unseen](../U/Unseen.md).

### Special equipment {#Special_equipment}

It is unknown whether Gandalf required his staff to exercise certain powers. At times it appeared to focus or extend his powers, such as its emanation of light. [Gríma Wormtongue](Gríma.md) tried to forbid Gandalf from bringing it into [Edoras](../E/Edoras.md) in a clear attempt to limit his powers.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">[46]</a></sup><sup class="references" style="margin: 0;line-height: 0.4em; padding-top: 0px !important; font-size: 70%;">:514</sup>

When he arrived in Middle-earth, Gandalf received the Elven ring [Narya](../N/Narya.md) from [Círdan](../C/Círdan.md).<sup id="cite_ref-Istari_9-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Istari-9">[9]</a></sup> Gandalf wore it from that time to the end of the Third Age, but how he used its powers is not known.

In [T.A.](../T/Third_Age.md) [2941](../T/Third_Age_2941.md), Gandalf acquired the Elven sword [Glamdring](Glamdring.md) from the treasure hoard of a band of trolls.<sup id="cite_ref-Mutton_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Mutton-17">[17]</a></sup> He continued to wield this weapon throughout *The Lord of the Rings*, in particular during his fight with the [Balrog](../D/Durin's_Bane.md) in Moria.

Throughout his time in Middle-earth Gandalf was often seen with a pipe which he used to smoke [Old Toby](../O/Old_Toby.md).
