<tr>
<td rowspan="2"><span class="plainlinks"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="../../file/Q/Quenya_-_Olórin.mp3.md" title="File:Quenya - Olórin.mp3"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/f/f2/Sound-icon.png/38px-Sound-icon.png" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="38" height="29" class="mw-file-element" srcset="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/f/f2/Sound-icon.png/57px-Sound-icon.png 1.5x, https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/thumb/f/f2/Sound-icon.png/76px-Sound-icon.png 2x" data-file-width="128" data-file-height="96"/></a></span></span></td>
<td style="text-align:center"><b><a href="../../file/Q/Quenya_-_Olórin.mp3.md" title="File:Quenya - Olórin.mp3">File:Quenya - Olórin.mp3</a></b></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td style="font-size: small; padding-bottom: 3px; text-align:center;">By <a href="https://tolkiengateway.net/wiki/User:Gilgamesh" title="User:Gilgamesh">Gilgamesh</a>. (<a class="extiw" href="http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_help" title="wikipedia:Media help">Media help</a>)</td>
</tr>
</tbody></table>
</div>

*Gandalf* is a [Norse](../N/North_Germanic_languages.md) name and supposedly means "Elvish wight with a (magic) staff", for it contains the element *gandr* ("a staff, especially one used in 'magic'"). It is a substitution in the English narrative for an unknown [Westron](../W/Westron.md) name (on the same lines as the treatment of Hobbit and Dwarf names).<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">[47]</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">[note 1]</a></sup>

The Men of the North called him so because they incorrectly thought that he was of Elven-kind<sup id="cite_ref-essay_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-essay-49">[48]</a></sup> and because they knew his alliance and friendship with Elves well<sup id="cite_ref-long_note_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-long_note-50">[49]</a></sup>.

Gandalf is also the name of a dwarf in the [Völuspá](../V/Völuspá.md), one of the poems of the [Poetic Edda](../P/Poetic_Edda.md)<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">[50]</a></sup>, from where Tolkien got many of the Dwarven names used in his books.

## Other names {#Other_names}

<div class="tg-quote-container"><div class="tg-section-divider"><span>”</span></div><blockquote class="tg-quote noexcerpt">
<p>Many are my names in many countries. Mithrandir among the Elves, Tharkûn to the Dwarves; Olórin I was in my youth in the West that is forgotten, in the South Incánus, in the North Gandalf; to the East I go not.
</p>
<p class="tg-quote-footer"><cite><i><a href="../T/The_Two_Towers.md" title="The Two Towers">The Two Towers</a></i>, &#34;<a href="../T/The_Window_on_the_West.md" title="The Window on the West">The Window on the West</a>&#34;</cite></p>
</blockquote><div class="tg-section-divider"></div></div>

- **Greyhame**: Used in [Rohan](../R/Rohan.md),<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">[51]</a></sup> it was the equivalent in [Rohanese](../R/Rohanese.md) of ""Greycloak" which would have been *grēg-hama*.<sup id="cite_ref-nomen_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nomen-53">[52]</a></sup>

- <i><b>Incánus</b></i>: Gained during his long travels "in the south" (likely meaning no further south than [Gondor](Gondor.md) or the [Near Harad](../N/Near_Harad.md)<sup id="cite_ref-Istari_9-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Istari-9">[9]</a></sup>) of [Middle-earth](../M/Middle-earth.md) in the mid-[Third Age](../T/Third_Age.md).<sup id="cite_ref-TT_Window_p._670_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TT_Window_p._670-11">[11]</a></sup> Although *Incánus* seems to be of unclear language and meaning, a note in the [Thain's Book](../T/Thain's_Book.md) says it is a [Quenya](../Q/Quenya.md) form simply adapted from a word in the tongue of the [Haradrim](../H/Haradrim.md): *Inkā-nūsh* (or possibly *Inkā-nūs*), meaning "North-spy".<sup id="cite_ref-Istari_9-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Istari-9">[9]</a></sup> <br>*Incánus* in Latin means "grey-haired". However, [Christopher Tolkien](../C/Christopher_Tolkien.md) has noted that the "coincidence in form" might be an "accident",<sup id="cite_ref-Istari_9-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Istari-9">[9]</a></sup> his father wrote "Incánus Latin" in a later published manuscript,<sup id="cite_ref-PE1788_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PE1788-54">[53]</a></sup> suggesting that the similarity was perhaps more than a coincidence. <br>Tolkien also changed his mind several times about the meaning of *Incánus*, besides the etymology given above his other variations were:
    - An archaic [Quenya](../Q/Quenya.md) word meaning "Mind-ruler", from *in(id)*- ("mind") and *cáno* ("ruler, governor, chieftain").<sup id="cite_ref-Istari_9-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Istari-9">[9]</a></sup>
    - An adaptation from Quenya *incānus(se)*, meaning "mind mastership".<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">[54]</a></sup>
    - A possible [Westron](../W/Westron.md) invention meaning "Greymantle".<sup id="cite_ref-Istari_9-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Istari-9">[9]</a></sup> In a draft manuscript of *[The Lord of the Rings](../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings.md)*, different names used were *Forlong*&gt;&gt;*Fornold*&gt;&gt;*Incânus*.<sup id="cite_ref-WR2V_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WR2V-56">[55]</a></sup>

- **Láthspell**: The name given to Gandalf by [Gríma Wormtongue](Gríma.md) when the former arrived at [Meduseld](../M/Meduseld.md). It means "Ill-news".<sup id="cite_ref-King_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-King-57">[56]</a></sup> It is a modernized form of [Old English](../O/Old_English.md) *lad-spell* ("a painful grievous story") from *lad* ("causing hate, evil, injury") and *spell* ("story", "message").<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">[57]</a></sup> In earlier drafts, it is actually Théoden who refers to Gandalf as Láthspell.<sup id="cite_ref-TIKing_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TIKing-59">[58]</a></sup>

- <i><b>Mithrandir</b></i>: His [Sindarin](../S/Sindarin.md) name (<span title="Pronunciation in IPA" class="IPA">[miˈθrandir]</span>), used in [Gondor](Gondor.md) and meaning "Grey Pilgrim" or "Grey Wanderer", from *[mith](../M/Mith.md)* ("grey") + *[randir](../R/Randir.md)* ("pilgrim, wandering man").<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">[59]</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">[60]</a></sup>

- <i><b>Olórin</b></i>: His original name in [Valinor](../V/Valinor.md). It is [Quenya](../Q/Quenya.md), and its meaning is associated with the Quenya word [*olos* or *olor*](../O/Olos.md), meaning "dream" or "vision / of mind".<sup id="cite_ref-Istari_9-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Istari-9">[9]</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-PE1788_54-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PE1788-54">[53]</a></sup> In a draft manuscript of *[The Lord of the Rings](../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings.md)*, occurs the spelling *Olórion*.<sup id="cite_ref-WR2V_56-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WR2V-56">[55]</a></sup>

- **Stormcrow**: The name given to Gandalf by King [Théoden](../T/Théoden.md) in [Rohan](../R/Rohan.md), a reference to his arrival being associated with times of trouble.<sup id="cite_ref-King_57-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-King-57">[56]</a></sup>

- <i><b>Tharkûn</b></i>: The name given to Gandalf by the [Dwarves](../D/Dwarves.md).<sup id="cite_ref-TT_Window_p._670_11-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TT_Window_p._670-11">[11]</a></sup> *Tharkûn* is [Khuzdul](../K/Khuzdul.md), meaning either "Grey-man"<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">[61]</a></sup> or "Staff-man".<sup id="cite_ref-Istari_9-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Istari-9">[9]</a></sup> The word possibly derives from the unattested word *thark* ("staff") + a nominal ending *-ûn*.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">[62]</a></sup> In a draft manuscript of *[The Lord of the Rings](../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings.md)*, occurs the spelling *Sharkûn*.<sup id="cite_ref-WR2V_56-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WR2V-56">[55]</a></sup>

- **The White Rider**: The name given to him by Aragorn when he rides the great horse [Shadowfax](../S/Shadowfax.md).<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">[63]</a></sup>

## Other versions of the legendarium {#Other_versions_of_the_legendarium}

In early manuscripts of *[The Hobbit](../T/The_Hobbit.md)*, the name *[Bladorthin](../B/Bladorthin.md)* was used by Tolkien for the character who later would be named *Gandalf*. The name *Gandalf* was instead used for the character known as [Thorin](../T/Thorin.md) in the published works.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">[64]</a></sup>

## Inspiration {#Inspiration}

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/G/Georg_von_Rosen_-_Oden_som_vandringsman,_1886_(Odin,_the_Wanderer).jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/1/1d/Georg_von_Rosen_-_Oden_som_vandringsman%2C_1886_%28Odin%2C_the_Wanderer%29.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="250" height="367" class="mw-file-element" data-file-width="250" data-file-height="367"/></a><figcaption><i>Oden som vandringsman</i> (<i>Odin the Wanderer</i>) by Georg von Rosen</figcaption></figure>

<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a class="mw-file-description" href="../../file/J/Josef_Madlener_-_Der_Berggeist.jpg.md"><img src="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/images/9/9d/Josef_Madlener_-_Der_Berggeist.jpg" decoding="async" loading="lazy" width="292" height="399" class="mw-file-element" data-file-width="292" data-file-height="399"/></a><figcaption><i><a href="../D/Der_Berggeist.md" title="Der Berggeist">Der Berggeist</a></i> by <a href="../J/Josef_Madlener.md" title="Josef Madlener">Josef Madlener</a></figcaption></figure>

*Gandalfr* appears in the list of dwarves in the *[Völuspá](../V/Völuspá.md)* of the Elder Edda, the name means "Cane-elf". Tolkien took the name along with the other dwarves' names when he wrote *The Hobbit* in the 1930s. He came to regret the creation of this "rabble of eddaic-named dwarves \[...\] invented in an idle hour",<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">[65]</a></sup> since it forced him to come up with an explanation of why Old Norse names should be used in [Third Age](../T/Third_Age.md) Middle-earth. He solved the dilemma in 1942 by the explanation that [Old Norse](../O/Old_Norse.md) was a *translation* of the [language of Dale](../L/Language_of_Dale.md). The figure of Gandalf has other influences from Germanic mythology, particularly Odin in his incarnation as "the Wanderer", an old man with one eye, a long white beard, a wide brimmed hat, and a staff: Tolkien states that he thinks of Gandalf as an *"Odinic wanderer"* in a letter of 1946.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">[66]</a></sup>

Tolkien had a postcard labelled *[Der Berggeist](../D/Der_Berggeist.md)* ("the mountain spirit"), and on the paper cover in which he kept it, he wrote "the origin of Gandalf" at some point. The postcard reproduces a painting of a bearded figure, sitting on a rock under a pine tree in a mountainous setting. He wears a wide-brimmed round hat and a long cloak and a white fawn is nuzzling his upturned hands. [Humphrey Carpenter](../H/Humphrey_Carpenter.md) in his 1977 biography said that Tolkien had bought the postcard during his 1911 holiday in Switzerland. However, Manfred Zimmerman discovered that the painting was by German artist Josef Madlener and dates to the late 1920s.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">[67]</a></sup> Carpenter concluded that Tolkien was probably mistaken about the origin of the postcard himself. Tolkien must have acquired the card at some time in the early 1930s, at a time when *The Hobbit* had already begun to take shape.

### Films {#Films}

<b>1967: <a href="../T/The_Hobbit_(1967_film).md" title="The Hobbit (1967 film)"><i>The Hobbit</i> (1967 film)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf&#39;s role is drastically reduced. He lives in a tower, where Thorin, the <a href="../M/Mika_Milovana.md" title="Mika Milovana">princess</a> and the guard meet him to discuss the killing of <a href="../S/Smaug.md" title="Smaug">Slag</a>. He introduces them to Bilbo, but does not go on the quest.<sup id="cite_ref-Deitch_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Deitch-69">[68]</a></sup></dd></dl>

<b>1971: <a href="../S/Sagan_om_Ringen_(1971_film).md" title="Sagan om Ringen (1971 film)"><i>Sagan om Ringen</i> (1971 film)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf is present at the <a href="../B/Bilbo_Baggins.md" title="Bilbo Baggins">Bilbo Baggins</a>&#39; <a href="../B/Bilbo&#39;s_Farewell_Party.md" title="Bilbo&#39;s Farewell Party">111th birthday party</a> and dines with <a href="../F/Frodo_Baggins.md" title="Frodo Baggins">Frodo</a>, <a href="../S/Samwise_Gamgee.md" title="Samwise Gamgee">Sam</a> <a href="../M/Meriadoc_Brandybuck.md" title="Meriadoc Brandybuck">Merry,</a> and <a href="../P/Peregrin_Took.md" title="Peregrin Took">Pippin</a>. Later, Frodo recieves <a href="../T/The_One_Ring.md" title="The One Ring">Bilbo&#39;s ring</a> as a birthday present and asks Gandalf about its purpose, having previously seen Biblo disappear with it. Gandalf tells Frodo of the forging of 20 magical rings and asks whether there are any marks on Bilbo&#39;s. Gandalf throws it into the fireplace, which reveals <a href="../R/Ring-inscription.md" title="Ring-inscription">text</a> on the ring, confirming Gandalf&#39;s suspicion that Bilbo&#39;s ring is indeed <a href="../T/The_One_Ring.md" title="The One Ring">The One Ring</a>. He encourages Frodo to bring the ring to <a href="../R/Rivendell.md" title="Rivendell">Rivendell</a> and participate in the <a href="../C/Council_of_Elrond.md" title="Council of Elrond">Council of Elrond</a>. He was imprisoned by <a href="../S/Saruman.md" title="Saruman">Saruman the Whute</a> on <a href="../O/Orthanc.md" title="Orthanc">his highest tower</a> and was able to escape by sending a message to the birds and being rescued by the <a class="mw-redirect" href="../E/Eagles.md" title="Eagles">Eagle</a> <a class="mw-redirect" href="Gwahir.md" title="Gwahir">Gwahir</a>. He flies to <a href="../R/Rohan.md" title="Rohan">Rohan</a> and is dropped off. Gandalf is given permission by <a href="../T/Théoden.md" title="Théoden">the king</a> to take a horse for himself. When looking for one, he discovers a a majestic white horse, named <a href="../S/Shadowfax.md" title="Shadowfax">Shadowfax</a> by the <a href="../M/Men.md" title="Men">Men</a> of Rohan. Gandalf notes his beauty, speed and the fact that that no man had ever rode him. Gandalf tames him and rides him to the Shire. He narrowly misses the Hobbits who are in the <a href="../O/Old_Forest.md" title="Old Forest">Old Forest</a>. Gandalf, unable to meet with the Frodo, and his Hobbit friends who agree to accompany him, at the <i><a class="mw-redirect" href="../P/Prancing_Pony.md" title="Prancing Pony">Prancing Pony Inn</a></i>, writes Frodo a letter, confirming that the man known as Strider is called <a href="../A/Aragorn.md" title="Aragorn">Aragorn</a> and is a man to be trusted. Finally arriving at the Prancing Pony, he hears word that Frodo had set off and he spends the night. The next day, <a href="../B/Bree.md" title="Bree">Bree</a> is stormed by <a href="../N/Nazgûl.md" title="Nazgûl">Black Riders</a> and Gandalf pursues them on Shadowfax to <a href="../W/Weathertop.md" title="Weathertop">Weathertop</a>. He arrives at Rivendell 3 days before Frodo. When Frodo arrives, he is comatosed by his encounter with the Black Riders. He greets Frodo when he recovers, 4 days after the Hobbit&#39;s arrival in Rivendell. He reunites with the Hobbits at the Council in Rivendell and tells the Council members of his journey. He agrees to join the <a href="../F/Fellowship_of_the_Ring.md" title="Fellowship of the Ring">Fellowship of the Ring</a>, which aims to bring the Ring to <a href="../M/Mordor.md" title="Mordor">Mordor</a> and destroy it. The Fellowship sets off with Gandalf leading the group. They take one final look at the House of Elrond and depart.</dd></dl>

<b>1977: <a href="../T/The_Hobbit_(1977_film).md" title="The Hobbit (1977 film)"><i>The Hobbit</i> (1977 film)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>The voice of Gandalf was provided by <a href="../J/John_Huston.md" title="John Huston">John Huston</a>.</dd></dl>

<b>1978: <a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings_(1978_film).md" title="The Lord of the Rings (1978 film)"><i>The Lord of the Rings</i> (1978 film)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf was voiced by <a href="../W/William_Squire.md" title="William Squire">William Squire</a>. <a href="../J/John_A._Neris.md" title="John A. Neris">John A. Neris</a> played him in the live-action filming used for rotoscoping.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">[69]</a></sup></dd></dl>

<b>1980: <a href="../T/The_Return_of_the_King_(1980_film).md" title="The Return of the King (1980 film)"><i>The Return of the King</i> (1980 film)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd><a href="../J/John_Huston.md" title="John Huston">John Huston</a> reprised his role as Gandalf. In this adaptation, the tale is told by a minstrel of <a href="Gondor.md" title="Gondor">Gondor</a>, yet in the story, Gandalf serves as the narrator. Gandalf the White is portrayed without difference from Gandalf the Grey, in <i>The Hobbit</i>.</dd></dl>

<b>1985: <a href="../T/The_Hobbit_(1985_television_film).md" title="The Hobbit (1985 television film)"><i>The Fairytale Adventures of Mr. Baggins, the Hobbit</i> (1985 film)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>Ivan Krasko played Gandalf.</dd></dl>

<b>2001: <i><a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_The_Fellowship_of_the_Ring.md" title="The Lord of the Rings: The Fellowship of the Ring">The Lord of the Rings: The Fellowship of the Ring</a></i>:</b>

<dl><dd><a href="../I/Ian_McKellen.md" title="Ian McKellen">Ian McKellen</a> was cast as Gandalf. He was nominated for an Academy Award for his portrayal.</dd></dl>

<b>2002: <i><a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_The_Two_Towers.md" title="The Lord of the Rings: The Two Towers">The Lord of the Rings: The Two Towers</a></i>:</b>

<dl><dd>Perhaps the most striking difference from all other adaptations is the difference between Gandalf the Grey and Gandalf the White. Where earlier no visible or audible difference was made, Gandalf the White is portrayed as much more virile, and with a shorter (and whiter) beard.</dd></dl>

<b>2003: <i><a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_The_Return_of_the_King.md" title="The Lord of the Rings: The Return of the King">The Lord of the Rings: The Return of the King</a></i>:</b>

<dl><dd>Because <a href="../I/Imrahil.md" title="Imrahil">Imrahil</a> had been cut from the film, it was up to Gandalf to lead the troops after the madness and death of <a href="../D/Denethor.md" title="Denethor">Denethor</a>.</dd></dl>

<b>2012-14: <a href="../T/The_Hobbit_(film_series).md" title="The Hobbit (film series)"><i>The Hobbit</i> (film series)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd><a href="../I/Ian_McKellen.md" title="Ian McKellen">Sir Ian McKellen</a> reprised his role as Gandalf the Grey.<sup id="cite_ref-PJCasting_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PJCasting-71">[70]</a></sup> A short description of Gandalf in <i>The Hobbit</i> films was released by the studio:</dd></dl>

<div class="tg-quote-container"><div class="tg-section-divider"><span>”</span></div><blockquote class="tg-quote noexcerpt">
<p>One of the most powerful Wizards in all Middle-earth, Gandalf the Grey joins the quest to reclaim the Dwarf Kingdom of Erebor and the great treasure that lies within its stone halls from a fire-breating dragon, Smaug the Terrible. Along the way, Gandalf finds evidence that an ancient evil may have found its way back into the world. In order to uncover the truth, Gandalf must leave his companions to fend for themselves – a journey that will take him into the darkest corners of Middle-earth where his worst suspicions are confirmed.
</p>
<p class="tg-quote-footer"><cite><a href="../W/Warner_Bros..md" title="Warner Bros.">Warner Bros.</a><sup id="cite_ref-HobbitApp_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HobbitApp-72">[71]</a></sup></cite></p>
</blockquote><div class="tg-section-divider"></div></div>

### Television {#Television}

<b>1993: <i><a href="../H/Hobitit.md" title="Hobitit">Hobitit</a></i>:</b>

<dl><dd><a class="new" href="https://tolkiengateway.net/w/index.php?title=Vesa_Vierikko&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" title="Vesa Vierikko (page does not exist)">Vesa Vierikko</a> played both Gandalf the Grey and Gandalf the White. He appeared in 6 of 8 episodes.</dd></dl>

<b>2024: <a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_The_Rings_of_Power.md" title="The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power">The <i>Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power</i></a></b>

<dl><dd><a href="../D/Daniel_Weyman.md" title="Daniel Weyman">Daniel Weyman</a> plays &#34;<a href="../T/The_Stranger.md" title="The Stranger">The Stranger</a>,&#34; who is eventually revealed to be Gandalf in a moment of foresight after being called &#34;Grand Elf&#34; by a group of departing <a href="../S/Stoors.md" title="Stoors">Stoors</a>.</dd></dl>

### Radio series {#Radio_series}

<b>1955: <a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings_(1955_radio_series).md" title="The Lord of the Rings (1955 radio series)"><i>The Lord of the Rings</i> (1955 radio series)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>The voice of Gandalf was provided by <a href="../N/Norman_Shelley.md" title="Norman Shelley">Norman Shelley</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">[72]</a></sup></dd></dl>

<b>1968: <a href="../T/The_Hobbit_(1968_radio_series).md" title="The Hobbit (1968 radio series)"><i>The Hobbit</i> (1968 radio series)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>The voice of Gandalf was provided by <a href="../H/Heron_Carvic.md" title="Heron Carvic">Heron Carvic</a>.</dd></dl>

<b>1979: <a href="../T/The_Hobbit_(1979_radio_series).md" title="The Hobbit (1979 radio series)"><i>The Hobbit</i> (1979 radio series)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>The voice of Gandalf was provided <a href="../B/Bernard_Mayes.md" title="Bernard Mayes">Bernard Mayes</a>.</dd></dl>

<b>1979: <a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings_(1979_radio_series).md" title="The Lord of the Rings (1979 radio series)"><i>The Lord of the Rings</i> (1979 radio series)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd><a href="../B/Bernard_Mayes.md" title="Bernard Mayes">Bernard Mayes</a> reprised his role as Gandalf.</dd></dl>

<b>1980: <a href="../D/Der_Hobbit_(1980_German_radio_series).md" title="Der Hobbit (1980 German radio series)"><i>Der Hobbit</i> (1980 German radio series)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf is played by Bernhard Minetti.</dd></dl>

<b>1981: <a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings_(1981_radio_series).md" title="The Lord of the Rings (1981 radio series)"><i>The Lord of the Rings</i> (1981 radio series)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd><a href="../M/Michael_Hordern.md" title="Michael Hordern">Michael Hordern</a> read the part of Gandalf. He had not read the book, and thought his agent made a mistake in telling him how many episodes he had to do. He did not know Gandalf&#39;s early death was temporary.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">[73]</a></sup></dd></dl>

<b>1989: <a href="../H/Hobit_(1989_Slovak_radio_series).md" title="Hobit (1989 Slovak radio series)"><i>Hobit</i> (1989 Slovak radio series)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>The voice of Gandalf is provided by Karol Machata.</dd></dl>

<b>1992: <a href="../D/Der_Herr_der_Ringe_(1992_German_radio_series).md" title="Der Herr der Ringe (1992 German radio series)"><i>Der Herr der Ringe</i> (1992 German radio series)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf is played by Manfed Steffen.</dd></dl>

<b>2001-2003: <a class="mw-redirect" href="../P/Pán_prsteňov_(2001-2003_Slovak_radio_series).md" title="Pán prsteňov (2001-2003 Slovak radio series)"><i>Pán prsteňov</i> (2001-2003 Slovak radio series)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>The voice of Gandalf is provided by Martin Huba.</dd></dl>

### Games {#Games}

<b>1982: <a href="../T/The_Hobbit_(1982_video_game).md" title="The Hobbit (1982 video game)"><i>The Hobbit</i> (1982 video game)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf can be met in several moments of the game&#39;s story line.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">[74]</a></sup></dd></dl>

<b>1990: <a href="../J/J.R.R._Tolkien&#39;s_The_Lord_of_the_Rings,_Vol._I_(1990_video_game).md" title="J.R.R. Tolkien&#39;s The Lord of the Rings, Vol. I (1990 video game)"><i>J.R.R. Tolkien&#39;s The Lord of the Rings, Vol. I</i> (1990 video game)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf can be &#34;recruited&#34; by <a href="../F/Frodo_Baggins.md" title="Frodo Baggins">Frodo Baggins</a> as a playable character.</dd></dl>

<b>1990: <i><a href="../J/J.R.R._Tolkien&#39;s_Riders_of_Rohan.md" title="J.R.R. Tolkien&#39;s Riders of Rohan">J.R.R. Tolkien&#39;s Riders of Rohan</a></i>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf is one of the main characters of the game and one of the several heroes of the <a href="../R/Rohan.md" title="Rohan">Rohan</a> faction.</dd></dl>

<b>1994: <a href="../J/J.R.R._Tolkien&#39;s_The_Lord_of_the_Rings,_Vol._I_(SNES).md" title="J.R.R. Tolkien&#39;s The Lord of the Rings, Vol. I (SNES)"><i>J.R.R. Tolkien&#39;s The Lord of the Rings, Vol. I</i> (SNES)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf can be acquired as a playable character.</dd></dl>

<b>2002: <a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_The_Fellowship_of_the_Ring_(video_game).md" title="The Lord of the Rings: The Fellowship of the Ring (video game)"><i>The Lord of the Rings: The Fellowship of the Ring</i> (video game)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd><a href="../T/Tom_Kane.md" title="Tom Kane">Tom Kane</a> provided the voice of Gandalf. He is a playable character in several levels, including the fight with <a href="../D/Durin&#39;s_Bane.md" title="Durin&#39;s Bane">Durin&#39;s Bane</a>.</dd></dl>

<b>2002: <a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_The_Two_Towers_(video_game).md" title="The Lord of the Rings: The Two Towers (video game)"><i>The Lord of the Rings: The Two Towers</i> (video game)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>The voice of Gandalf is provided by <a href="../I/Ian_McKellen.md" title="Ian McKellen">Ian McKellen</a>. Gandalf the Grey appears at the campsite of the <a href="../F/Fellowship_of_the_Ring.md" title="Fellowship of the Ring">fellowship</a> in <i>The Gates of Moria</i> and during the fight of <a href="../B/Balin&#39;s_Tomb.md" title="Balin&#39;s Tomb">Balin&#39;s Tomb</a> in <i>Balin&#39;s Tomb</i>. He latter reappears in the game as Gandalf the White at the start of the mission <i>The Plains of Rohan</i>, where he leaves Aragorn, Legolas and Gimli to search Éomer and his army. He also appears in some cutscenes.</dd></dl>

<b>2002: <a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_The_Two_Towers_(video_game).md#Game_Boy_Advance_version" title="The Lord of the Rings: The Two Towers (video game)"><i>The Lord of the Rings: The Two Towers</i> (Game Boy Advance)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>In the <a class="extiw" href="http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Game_Boy_Advance" title="wikipedia:Game Boy Advance">Game Boy Advance version</a> of <a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_The_Two_Towers_(video_game).md" title="The Lord of the Rings: The Two Towers (video game)">The Lord of the Rings: The Two Towers</a> Gandalf is a playable character. Gandalf&#39;s story starts with a conversation between Gandalf and <a href="../T/Théoden.md" title="Théoden">Théoden</a>, after which the story goes back to the <a href="../C/Caradhras.md" title="Caradhras">Caradhras</a>, where the Fellowship tries to travel over the  <a href="../M/Misty_Mountains.md" title="Misty Mountains">Misty Mountains</a>. When that road fails, the Fellowship goes back and travels through <a href="../M/Moria.md" title="Moria">Moria</a>. In Moria Gandalf and the other member of the Fellowship are separated. After finding <a href="../F/Frodo_Baggins.md" title="Frodo Baggins">Frodo</a>, <a href="../A/Aragorn.md" title="Aragorn">Aragorn</a> and <a href="Gimli.md" title="Gimli">Gimli</a> the fellowship is attacked by a <a href="../C/Cave-trolls.md" title="Cave-trolls">Cave-troll</a> and <a href="../O/Orcs.md#Orcs_and_Goblins" title="Orcs">Goblins</a>. After Gandalf defeats the Cave-troll the Fellowship has to flee for the <a href="../B/Balrogs.md" title="Balrogs">Balrog</a>. When they reach the <a href="../B/Bridge_of_Khazad-dûm.md" title="Bridge of Khazad-dûm">Bridge of Khazad-dûm</a> a cutscene is shown, in which Gandalf destroys the bridge and falls with the Balrog in the depths. After the fall Gandalf has to follow the footsteps of the Balrog through the depths of Moria and the <a href="../E/Endless_Stair.md" title="Endless Stair">Endless Stair</a> until he reaches <a href="../D/Durin&#39;s_Tower.md" title="Durin&#39;s Tower">Durin&#39;s Tower</a>, where he has to defeat the Balrog.</dd></dl>

<dl><dd>After being revived he returns as Gandalf the White in Fangorn, where he defeats several <a href="../T/Trolls.md" title="Trolls">Trolls</a> and <a href="../C/Crebain.md" title="Crebain">Crebain</a>. After defeating a stronger Forrest troll, named Fangorn troll, Gandalf meets Aragorn, Legolas and Gimli. After a cutscene, the game moves to <a href="../E/Edoras.md" title="Edoras">Edoras</a>. Gandalf heals the corrupted Théoden in Edoras, and then travels through <a href="../R/Rohan.md" title="Rohan">Rohan</a> in search of <a href="../_/Éomer.md" title="Éomer">Éomer</a>. Gandalf convinces Éomer to lead his army to <a href="../H/Helm&#39;s_Deep.md" title="Helm&#39;s Deep">Helm&#39;s Deep</a>.</dd></dl>

<dl><dd>The game ends with the <a href="../B/Battle_of_the_Hornburg.md" title="Battle of the Hornburg">Battle of Helm&#39;s Deep</a>, in which Gandalf and Éomer fight alongside Aragorn, Legolas, Gimli and <a href="../_/Éowyn.md" title="Éowyn">Éowyn</a> while protecting the wall, the <a href="../H/Hornburg.md" title="Hornburg">Hornburg</a> and the <a href="Glittering_Caves.md" title="Glittering Caves">Glittering Caves</a>. In the final level Gandalf has to destroy the siege weapons that bombard the Hornburg. The game ends with a conversation between Gandalf, Aragorn, Gimli and Théoden.</dd></dl>

<b>2003: <a href="../T/The_Hobbit_(2003_video_game).md" title="The Hobbit (2003 video game)"><i>The Hobbit</i> (2003 video game)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd><a href="../J/Jim_Ward.md" title="Jim Ward">Jim Ward</a> provided the voice of Gandalf. He is shown with actual <a href="../M/Magic.md" title="Magic">magic</a>: he can shoot blue bolts of lightning from his staff.</dd></dl>

<b>2003: <a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_The_Return_of_the_King_(video_game).md" title="The Lord of the Rings: The Return of the King (video game)"><i>The Lord of the Rings: The Return of the King</i> (video game)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf is the narrator, and a playable character. The game is built around him; the cut scene narration shows how he planned for the <a href="../W/War_of_the_Ring.md" title="War of the Ring">War of the Ring</a>. He has several missions, collectively called the &#34;Path of the Wizard&#34;. The first is the <a href="../B/Battle_of_the_Hornburg.md" title="Battle of the Hornburg">Battle of Helm&#39;s Deep</a>, which serves as a training mission. He has to fight <a class="mw-redirect" href="../U/Uruk-Hai~9489.md" title="Uruk-Hai">Uruk-Hai</a> in the forest of <a href="../H/Huorns.md" title="Huorns">Huorns</a> and <a href="../E/Ents.md" title="Ents">Ents</a> in &#34;The Road to Isengard&#34;. After that, the missions move to <a href="../M/Minas_Tirith.md" title="Minas Tirith">Minas Tirith</a>: the first mission, &#34;Top of the Wall&#34;, has Gandalf warding off ladders and siege towers. In the second, &#34;Courtyard&#34;, he has to protect civilians from the invading Orcs. His last playing mission is the <a href="../B/Battle_of_the_Morannon.md" title="Battle of the Morannon">Battle of the Black Gate</a>.</dd></dl>

<b>2003: <a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_The_Return_of_the_King_(video_game).md" title="The Lord of the Rings: The Return of the King (video game)"><i>The Lord of the Rings: The Return of the King</i> (Game Boy Advance)</a>:</b>

<dl><dd>Just like on the console version of the game, Gandalf is a playable character. He has many missions, collectively called the &#34;The Journey of the Wizard&#34;. He starts in a flooded <a href="../I/Isengard.md" title="Isengard">Isengard</a>, where he has to kill several <a href="../U/Uruk-hai.md" title="Uruk-hai">Uruk-hai</a> and <a href="../C/Crebain.md" title="Crebain">Crebain</a> before he confronts <a href="../S/Saruman.md" title="Saruman">Saruman</a>. After the defeat of Saruman he travels to <a href="../M/Minas_Tirith.md" title="Minas Tirith">Minas Tirith</a> through Rohan and the Misty Mountains. At the <a href="../P/Pelennor_Fields.md" title="Pelennor Fields">Pelennor Fields</a> Gandalf has to protect the <a class="mw-redirect" href="../B/Beacons_of_Gondor.md" title="Beacons of Gondor">beacons</a>, so Rohan can be warned. During the <a href="../B/Battle_of_the_Pelennor_Fields.md" title="Battle of the Pelennor Fields">Battle of Minas Tirith</a> he has to protect the gate, before making his way to the <a href="../H/House_of_the_Stewards.md" title="House of the Stewards">Steward&#39;s Tomb</a>. Here Gandalf has to kill Denethor, before he burns <a href="../F/Faramir.md" title="Faramir">Faramir</a>. After the battle Gandalf travels through <a href="../I/Ithilien.md" title="Ithilien">Ithilien</a> to the <a href="../B/Black_Gate.md" title="Black Gate">Black Gate</a>, where he confronts a Nazgûl.</dd></dl>

<b>2004: <i><a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_The_Third_Age.md" title="The Lord of the Rings: The Third Age">The Lord of the Rings: The Third Age</a></i>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf, voiced by <a href="../I/Ian_McKellen.md" title="Ian McKellen">Ian McKellen</a>, is a character in <a href="../E/Electronic_Arts.md" title="Electronic Arts">EA&#39;s</a> &#34;alternative <a href="../F/Fellowship_of_the_Ring.md" title="Fellowship of the Ring">fellowship</a>&#34; game. In his battle against Durin&#39;s Bane, he is aided by the main characters.</dd></dl>

<b>2004: <i><a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_War_of_the_Ring.md" title="The Lord of the Rings: War of the Ring">The Lord of the Rings: War of the Ring</a></i>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf is voiced by <a href="../S/Steven_M._Kramer.md" title="Steven M. Kramer">Steven M. Kramer</a>, renowned for playing older mentor types.</dd></dl>

<b>2004: <i><a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_The_Battle_for_Middle-earth.md" title="The Lord of the Rings: The Battle for Middle-earth">The Lord of the Rings: The Battle for Middle-earth</a></i>:</b>

<dl><dd>In non-storyline skirmishes Gandalf is the hero of the Gondor faction, who possesses several magical abilities. At first, he appears as Gandalf &#34;the Grey&#34;, and becomes &#34;Gandalf the White&#34; after reaching level 5 (out of 10). Gandalf also narrates the prologue scene, repeating almost word for word the lines said by Galadriel in the <i><a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_The_Fellowship_of_the_Ring.md" title="The Lord of the Rings: The Fellowship of the Ring">The Lord of the Rings: The Fellowship of the Ring</a></i>.</dd></dl>

<dl><dd>His involvement in the storyline is notable for major differences from the original tale. First of all, Gandalf survives his encounter with Balrog, defeating him at the Bridge of Khazad-dum and continues the journey with the Fellowship - by the time of arrival in Lothlorien he already appears as Gandalf the White. He is present during the ambush at <a href="../A/Amon_Hen.md" title="Amon Hen">Amon Hen</a>, where thanks to his involvement Boromir is saved from certain death. Merry and Pippin are captured regardless and Aragorn, Legolas, Gimli and Boromir give chase, while Gandalf departs them to see to dealings in Rohan, including freeing of King Théoden from the spell. Following the Battle of Helm&#39;s Deep he travels to Minas Tirith along with both Pippin and Boromir, where they participate in the Siege of the city.</dd></dl>

<b>2006: <i><a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_The_Battle_for_Middle-earth_II.md" title="The Lord of the Rings: The Battle for Middle-earth II">The Lord of the Rings: The Battle for Middle-earth II</a></i>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf is a hero for the &#34;Men of the West&#34; faction. Similar to the first game, he appears as Gandalf the Grey initially and Gandalf the White after level 5. He plays no part in the main storyline, but appears in the alternate &#34;evil&#34; campaign: after Sauron reclaims the Ring and lays waste to southern lands, Gandalf and few other survivors meet their end during a last stand in Rivendell.</dd></dl>

<b>2007: <i><a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings_Online.md" title="The Lord of the Rings Online">The Lord of the Rings Online</a></i>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf is a non-playable character voiced by <a href="../H/Harry_Chase.md" title="Harry Chase">Harry Chase</a>, who narrates cutscenes and instances throughout the original game, before the first expansion. He first meets the Dwarven characters in Ered Luin in <a href="../T/Third_Age.md" title="Third Age">T.A.</a> <a href="../T/Third_Age_2941.md" title="Third Age 2941">2941</a>, shortly before the Quest for Erebor. Later, characters of all races meet him in Bree in October of <a href="../T/Third_Age.md" title="Third Age">T.A.</a> <a href="../T/Third_Age_3018.md" title="Third Age 3018">3018</a>, but he is too busy with concerns for Frodo and the Ring and merely sends them away to find Radagast. Finally, the players have a chance to have a proper conversation with him in Rivendell, following the Council of Elrond. During that time, Gandalf is involved in several quests, including helping the player wreck havoc among the Goblins stirring at the <a href="../H/High_Pass.md" title="High Pass">High Pass</a>. After the Fellowship leaves Rivendell, players are able to retrace many of their steps, including the marks left by Gandalf such as the Burnt Top in <a href="../E/Eregion.md" title="Eregion">Eregion</a> and the remains of his fire at the pass of <a href="../C/Caradhras.md" title="Caradhras">Caradhras</a>. Later, Lady <a href="Galadriel.md" title="Galadriel">Galadriel</a> bids the player to find the signs of Gandalf following his fall from the Bridge of Khazad-dum - said signs include his burnt hat at the Foundations of Stone in Moria and the signs of his struggle with the Balrog at the <a href="../E/Endless_Stair.md" title="Endless Stair">Endless Stair</a> - those allow Galadriel to discern that Mithrandir is not truly dead. Players later meet him as Gandalf the White in <a href="../C/Caras_Galadhon.md" title="Caras Galadhon">Caras Galadhon</a> shortly after the Fellowship has departed Lothlorien. During that time, he engages into a spiritual battle with a Gaunt-Lord Gortheron the Doom-Caller; his display of his new abilities encourages the band of Free People players and allows them to defeat the servant of Sauron.</dd></dl>

<dl><dd>Gandalf is also present in several historic &#34;session plays&#34;, during which players witness the important events their characters were not present for. Such events include Gandalf infiltrating <a href="../D/Dol_Guldur.md" title="Dol Guldur">Dol Guldur</a> with the help of an Elf named Raddir, first meeting between Gandalf and Aragorn on the outskirts of Lothlorien and Gandalf&#39;s imprisonment atop Orthanc by Saruman.</dd></dl>

<b>2009: <i><a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_Conquest.md" title="The Lord of the Rings: Conquest">The Lord of the Rings: Conquest</a></i>:</b>

<dl><dd><a href="../M/Martin_Jarvis.md" title="Martin Jarvis">Martin Jarvis</a> provides the voice of Gandalf. He is a playable mage hero and has three special powers: &#34;Healing Wisdom&#34;, &#34;You Shall not Pass!&#34; and &#34;Cleansing Fire&#34;. In the good campaign he appears at the end of the Isengard mission, where he has to kill Saruman in <a href="../O/Orthanc.md" title="Orthanc">Orthanc</a>. In Moria he can be played to destroy the <a class="mw-redirect" href="../B/Balrog.md" title="Balrog">Balrog</a> and in Minas Tirith he has to defend the gates of the second ring. He is also one of the four playable heroes in the last mission, <a class="mw-redirect" href="../T/The_Battle_of_the_Morannon.md" title="The Battle of the Morannon">the battle of the Black Gate</a>. He appears as well at the end of the last mission, the Shire, of the evil campaign, in which he is defeated by <a href="../S/Sauron.md" title="Sauron">Sauron</a>.</dd>
<dd>He is also playable in the Shire, Isengard, Minas Tirith and Moria with the <i>Conquest</i> Mode, in Isengard and Minas Tirith in the <i>Team Deathmatch</i> mode, the citadel of Minas Tirith in <i>Capture the Ring</i> mode, and at the Black Gate, Minas Morgul, Mount Doom and the Shire during <i>Hero Team Deathmatch</i> mode.</dd></dl>

<b>2011: <i><a href="../T/The_Lord_of_the_Rings%253A_War_in_the_North.md" title="The Lord of the Rings: War in the North">The Lord of the Rings: War in the North</a></i>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf is voiced by <a href="../T/Tom_Kane.md" title="Tom Kane">Tom Kane</a>, who also narrates the introduction of the game. He tells that heroes like &#34;<a href="../A/Aragorn.md" title="Aragorn">Aragorn the King</a>&#34;, &#34;<a href="../F/Frodo_Baggins.md" title="Frodo Baggins">Frodo the Ringbearer</a>&#34; and &#34;<b>Gandalf the Wizard</b>&#34; are rightly honoured, but that without a few heroes - <a href="../E/Eradan_(video_game_character).md" title="Eradan (video game character)">Eradan</a>, <a href="../A/Andriel.md" title="Andriel">Andriel</a> and <a href="../F/Farin_(video_game_character).md" title="Farin (video game character)">Farin</a> - the north of <a href="../M/Middle-earth.md" title="Middle-earth">Middle-earth</a> would have been lost.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">[75]</a></sup></dd>
<dd>He also appears in <a href="../R/Rivendell.md" title="Rivendell">Rivendell</a> later in the game. Players can interact with him and learn of various important events, yet the conversations do not unlock any side-quests and do not affect the main story in any way.</dd></dl>

<b>2012: <i><a href="Guardians_of_Middle-earth.md" title="Guardians of Middle-earth">Guardians of Middle-earth</a></i>:</b>

<dl><dd>Gandalf is a mage-type &#34;guardian&#34; with four abilities: <i>Narya&#39;s Power</i>, <i>Flame of Anor</i>, <i>Fireworks</i> and <i>Gandalf&#39;s Might</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">[76]</a></sup></dd></dl>

<b>2025: <i><a href="../T/Tales_of_the_Shire%253A_A_%2522The_Lord_of_the_Rings%2522_Game.md" title="Tales of the Shire: A &#34;The Lord of the Rings&#34; Game">Tales of the Shire: A &#34;The Lord of the Rings&#34; Game</a></i>:</b>

<dl><dd>At the beginning of the game, Gandalf rides in his cart and discovers the player <a href="../H/Hobbits.md" title="Hobbits">Hobbit</a>, sleeping on a log. He wakes them and helps them up, giving them a ride in his cart to <a href="../B/Bywater.md" title="Bywater">Bywater</a>. He gives the Hobbit <a href="../B/Bilbo_Baggins.md" title="Bilbo Baggins">Bilbo Baggins</a>&#39; map of Bywater and begins to depart. The Hobbit asks him his name and he tells them &#34;<a href="../A/An_Unexpected_Party.md" title="An Unexpected Party">I am Gandalf, and Gandalf means me.</a>&#34; before farewelling them and continuing on the road.</dd></dl>

## Notes {#Notes}

<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-48"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><a href="#cite_ref-48">↑</a></span> <span class="reference-text">Since the name <i>Gandalf</i> is attributed to &#34;the North&#34; in general, it probably represents a Westron name, but one made up of elements not derived from Elvish tongues.</span>
</li>
</ol></div>

<span></span>
